## All-In-One Security (AIOS)

**Source:** [https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/all-in-one-security-aios/](https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/all-in-one-security-aios/)

![All-In-One Security (AIOS)](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/02/aios-logo.png)If you are using the [All-In-One Security (AIOS) – Security and Firewall](https://wordpress.org/plugins/all-in-one-wp-security-and-firewall/) plugin, make sure to **not** disable access to the WordPress REST API.

This plugin has a setting to disable the WordPress REST API access when you are not logged in. Enabling this setting will cause a 
```
500
```

 server error on refresh (when using facets), which causes FacetWP to malfunction.

To disable this setting, go to:

AIOS > Firewall > PHP rules > WP REST API > Disallow unauthorized REST requests

![Disable the 'Disallow unauthorized REST requests' setting in the AIOS plugin.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/aios-plugin-dont-disable-wp-rest-api.png)Disable the “Disallow unauthorized REST requests” setting in the AIOS plugin.
Tip:If you want to protect specific WordPress REST API endpoints (like [FacetWP’s endpoints](/help-center/developers/facetwp-rest-api/)) from being accessed by non-logged-in users, check out the [WordPress REST API Authentication](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/wordpress-rest-api-authentication/) plugin. Or you can [protect the entire WordPress API from non-logged-in users](/help-center/developers/facetwp-rest-api/#restrict-the-rest-api-and-facet-filtering-to-authenticated-users). In both scenarios, because FacetWP needs its own 
```
/facetwp/v1/refresh
```

 endpoint to function, your facets will then only work for logged-in users. Additionally, you’ll need to add [this fix to pass authentication data through REST API requests](/how-to-pass-authentication-data-through-rest-api-requests/), to fix facet filtering.
    
## See also

    - [The FacetWP REST API](https://facetwp.com/help-center/developers/facetwp-rest-api/)
- [Using FacetWP with the WordPress REST API Authentication plugin](https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/wordpress-rest-api-authentication/)
- [How to pass authentication data through REST API requests](https://facetwp.com/how-to-pass-authentication-data-through-rest-api-requests/)

## All in One SEO (Pro)

**Source:** [https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/all-in-one-seo-pro/](https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/all-in-one-seo-pro/)

If you are using [All in One SEO](https://wordpress.org/plugins/all-in-one-seo-pack/) or [All in One SEO Pro](https://aioseo.com/) together with FacetWP, be aware of the following issues and incompatibilities:

## Fix no results after filtering

![All in One SEO logo](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/03/aioseo-logo.png)

In [specific situations](#triggers), AIOSEO (Pro) will cause a JSON error in the AJAX refresh data that is returned to FacetWP after filtering. This will cause FacetWP to be unable to process the response, resulting in a “no results” message, or simply no posts showing up.

Fortunately [there is an easy fix](#the-fix-add-title-tag-support).

### Triggers

The following factors all need to be present to trigger this error:

1. All in One SEO (Pro) is installed.
2. As listing template you are using a [WP archive page](/help-center/listing-templates/wp-archive-page/), a [custom WP_Query](/help-center/listing-templates/custom-wp-query/), or a [page builder query](/help-center/listing-templates/using-a-page-builder/). The issue does not happen when you are using a [Listing Builder listing template](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/).
3. Your active theme does **not** have [support for the ‘title-tag’ feature](#how-to-test-for-title-tag-theme-support). This causes AIOSEO to use PHP output buffering to customize the theme’s 
```
<title>
```

 tag, which in turn is the cause of these issues.

### How to test for ‘title-tag’ theme support

To quickly test if your theme has support for the 
```
title-tag
```

 feature, you can temporarily add the following to your theme’s functions.php:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More info// Remove after testing
add_action( 'after_setup_theme', function() {
  var_dump( current_theme_supports( 'title-tag' ) ); // outputs bool(false) if 'title-tag' is not supported, or bool(true) if 'title-tag' is supported
} );
```

If you see the output 
```
bool(false)
```

, your theme does not have this feature, and you can apply [the fix below](#the-fix-add-title-tag-support) to add support for it.

If it outputs 
```
bool(true)
```

, your theme does already support this feature, which means that the “no results” issue is **not** caused by the AIOSEO plugin.

Important:Make sure to **remove the above code immediately** after confirmation, as it will cause the same type of JSON error as is causing the issue, which may confuse further testing and fixing.

### What is the ‘title-tag’ feature?

Before WordPress version 4.1, the only way to dynamically generate 
```
<title>
```

 tags based on the page/post title was by using the [wp_title() function](https://developer.wordpress.org/reference/functions/wp_title/) in the 
```
<head>
```

 section of the theme. To customize its output, theme authors could use the  [wp_title hook](https://developer.wordpress.org/reference/hooks/wp_title/).

In version 4.1, WordPress [introduced a new way of generating title tags](https://make.wordpress.org/core/2014/10/29/title-tags-in-4-1/), by letting WP Core handle their generation and output. Since then, the 
```
<title>
```

 tag can be customized with a range of new filters, like the [document_title_parts hook](https://developer.wordpress.org/reference/hooks/document_title_parts/). The 
```
wp_title()
```

 function was deprecated (but later [reinstated again](https://make.wordpress.org/core/2015/10/20/document-title-in-4-4/)). The use of 
```
wp_title()
```

 is now discouraged.

By declaring 
```
'title-tag'
```

 support, themes acknowledge that they are not defining titles on their own and WordPress can safely add them without duplication.

### The fix: add ‘title-tag’ support

Most modern themes have 
```
'title-tag'
```

 support. If your theme [does not have it](#how-to-test-for-title-tag-theme-support), you can enable it by adding the following code to your theme’s functions.php:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More infoadd_action( 'after_setup_theme', function() {
  add_theme_support( 'title-tag' );
} );
```

If you do this, you have to remove any uses of 
```
wp_title()
```

 in the 
```
<head>
```

 section (generally in your theme’s header.php file), to prevent duplicate title tags.

#### Alternative fix: disable title rewrites by All in One SEO

If you don’t want to [add ‘title-tag’ support to your theme](#the-fix-add-title-tag-support), an alternative fix is to disable 
```
<title>
```

 rewrites by AIOSEO. You can limit this to your facet page like this:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More infoadd_filter( 'aioseo_disable_title_rewrites', function( $disabled ) {
  if ( is_page('my-facet-page') ) {
    return true; // disable AOISEO title rewrites
  }
  return false;
} );
```

Or you can apply it site-wide:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More infoadd_filter( 'aioseo_disable_title_rewrites', function( $disabled ) {
  return true; // disable AOISEO title rewrites
} );
```

Be careful with this, as it could potentially hurt your SEO.

## Fix a “FacetWP was unable to auto-detect the post listing” error

If you see “FacetWP was unable to auto-detect the post listing” after using facets, make sure to **disable the “Run Shortcodes” setting**, located under All in One SEO > Search Appearance > Advanced. [AIOSEO shortcode parsing](https://aioseo.com/docs/running-shortcodes/) breaks FacetWP’s query detection.

![Disable shortcode parsing by AIOSEO.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/08/aioseo-run-shortcodes.png)Disable shortcode parsing by AIOSEO.
Alternatively, if you have a reason to enable this setting, you can exclude 
```
facetwp
```

 shortcodes from being parsed by AIOSEO, [using the aioseo_conflicting_shortcodes hook](https://aioseo.com/docs/aioseo_conflicting_shortcodes/). To do so, add the following code to your (child) theme’s functions.php:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More infoadd_filter( 'aioseo_conflicting_shortcodes', function( $conflictingShortcodes ) {
  $conflictingShortcodes = array_merge( $conflictingShortcodes, [
    'FacetWP' => '[facetwp]',
  ] );
  return $conflictingShortcodes;
} );
```

## Fix AIOSEO URL redirects in older AIOSEO versions

In some older versions of AIOSEO there is a setting called “Remove Query Args”, located under [Crawl Cleanup](https://aioseo.com/docs/crawl-cleanup-best-practices/), found in the section: Search Appearance > Advanced > Crawl Cleanup.

If you have that setting, make sure to **disable** it:

![Disable AIOSEO's Crawl Cleanup > Remove Query Args setting under Search Appearance > Advanced.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/08/aioseo-crawl-cleanup-remove-query-args.png)Disable AIOSEO’s Crawl Cleanup > Remove Query Args setting under Search Appearance > Advanced.
If this setting is enabled, query arguments will be automatically removed from the URL, and the page will get a 
```
301
```

 redirect to the URL without any facet selections. This breaks facet filtering.

Note that query arguments are only removed by this setting when you are **not logged in**.

In newer AIOSEO versions, the above-mentioned setting is replaced by a “Query Arg Monitoring” section. If you use this, make sure not to block any query arguments that are used on pages with facets.

- [Using a page builder](https://facetwp.com/help-center/listing-templates/using-a-page-builder/)
- [Using the Listing Builder](https://facetwp.com/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/)

## Beaver Builder

**Source:** [https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/beaver-builder/](https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/beaver-builder/)

This add-on lets you display facets alongside [a range of Beaver Builder listing modules](#supported-modules). These modules are available when using [Beaver Builder](https://www.wpbeaverbuilder.com/) and (optionally) [Beaver Themer](https://www.wpbeaverbuilder.com/beaver-themer/), or one of the mentioned Beaver Builder add-on plugins.

With this add-on installed, FacetWP also adds [three modules of its own](#facetwp-modules), for adding [facets](/help-center/facets/), [Listing Builder listings](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/), and three FacetWP-related buttons.

## Supported modules

The following modules are supported and can be used to [create FacetWP compatible listings](#usage). Note that all modules [need to be enabled for FacetWP](#usage) to be filterable by facets.

| Plugin | Module name |
| --- | --- |
| Beaver Builder | Posts
Notes:

The Posts module works with Columns, Masonry, Gallery and List layouts (set under Layout).
It also works with the Equal Heights setting which can be set for Columns and Masonry layouts.

WooCommerce
Notes:

The WooCommerce module only works with the setting: Layout > Multiple Products. For the Products Source child setting, all options are supported.
The WooCommerce module will show gaps in the layout when using a Load More Pager facet. See below for how to fix this. |
| Beaver Builder  with Beaver Themer | Loop
Required versions:

Beaver Builder v2.10.1.2+
Beaver Themer v1.5.2.3+
FacetWP Beaver Builder add-on v1.5+ |
| Ultimate Addons for Beaver Builder | Advanced Posts
Notes:

The Advanced Posts module works with Grid, Masonry, Feeds, and Carousel layouts (set under General > Post Appearance). 
This module also works with the Equal Height Boxes and Masonry Equal Height settings, which can be set under the Style tab for Grid, Masonry, and Carousel layouts.
The Carousel layout does not work with a Load More Pager facet.
The Taxonomy Filter feature (available in the Query tab for Grid and Masonry layouts) does not work with FacetWP.

Woo – Products (with WooCommerce)
Notes:

The Woo Products module works with Grid and Carousel layouts (set under General > Layout).
The Carousel layout does not work with a Load More Pager facet. |
| PowerPack/WooPack for Beaver Builder | Content Grid
Notes:

Only supported with the setting: Content > Layout > Grid. Carousel layout is not supported.

Product Grid (with WooCommerce) |

Note:Not all module features and settings are supported. [See below](#known-issues-incompatibilities-and-unsupported-module-settings) for known issues, incompatibilities, and unsupported module settings.
The FacetWP Beaver Builder add-on (v1.5+) adds the following modules:

| Plugin | Module name |
| --- | --- |
| FacetWP | Facet
Listing
Button |

Note: In versions **older than v1.5** of the add-on, there were *seven* FacetWP modules. The Template module is now named Listing module. The Pager, Per Page, Counts, Selections, and Sort modules are now **deprecated** and will be **non-functional after updating**. We recommend [migrating them to the updated Facet module](#migrate-deprecated-facetwp-modules). If you want to keep using these old modules, it is possible to [re-enable them with a hook](#re-enable-deprecated-facetwp-modules).

## Usage

Download the Beaver Builder add-on from [your account](/account/). After installation and activation, add one of the [supported modules](#supported-modules) to you page and [enable it for FacetWP](#enable-facetwp-on-the-module). Or add a [FacetWP Listing module](#the-facetwp-listing-module) and select one of your previously created FacetWP [listing templates](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/). Then, add some facets with a [Facet module](#the-facetwp-facet-module).

### Enable FacetWP on the module

![How to enable FacetWP in a supported Beaver Builder listing module.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-loop-module-facetwp-setting-enabled.png)How to enable FacetWP in a supported Beaver Builder listing module.
All supported Beaver Builder listing modules (except [FacetWP’s own modules](#facetwp-modules)) will display a “FacetWP” setting in their settings. Enable this setting on the module that you want to be filtered with the [facets on the page](#the-facetwp-facet-module).

After [choosing a query source](#choose-a-query-source) and saving, the module will render in the editor.

Important:Make sure there is **only one FacetWP-enabled listing module on a page**. Using multiple FacetWP-enabled listing modules per page is **not** supported.

### Choose a query source

![The Source setting in a supported Beaver Builder listing modules.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-loop-module-source-setting.png)The Source setting in a supported Beaver Builder listing modules.
In the module’s “Source” setting, choose a query source that queries posts (of any post type). **Other source options like taxonomy queries or ACF repeaters are not supported**, as FacetWP cannot filter those.

Choose “Custom Query” to create your own custom query, or “Main Query” if the module is in an archive template. The WooCommerce modules will show product-related options to use.

Note that in [archive templates](#using-modules-on-archive-templates), facets may work even without enabling the “FacetWP” setting, because of FacetWP’s query autodetection. However, to prevent issues with pagination, make sure to always enable the “FacetWP” setting, also in archive templates.

In [some of the supported modules](#migrate-facetwp-listing-templates-in-the-source-dropdown), you may also see your FacetWP [Listing Builder listings](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/) in the “Source” dropdown. This happens if you were already using one before updating to v1.5+ of the add-on, or if you [explicitly re-enabled them](#re-enable-facetwp-listing-templates-in-the-source-dropdown). After choosing one, the module will use the query settings of that listing template (but not its layout and styling settings). However, [using a FacetWP listing template like this is deprecated](#migrate-facetwp-listing-templates-in-the-source-dropdown), and you will see a deprecation message in the module settings. If you need a FacetWP listing to be displayed, including its layout and styling, we recommend using a [FacetWP Listing module instead](#the-facetwp-listing-module) (available in v1.5+ of the add-on).

### Add one or more facets

After [adding a FacetWP-enabled module](#usage), add some facets to the page that will filter this module. The recommended way is to add them with a [Facet module](#the-facetwp-facet-module). You can also [use a shortcode in an HTML module](#add-facetwp-shortcodes-with-an-html-module).

## FacetWP modules

![Available FacetWP modules in the Beaver Builder add-on v1.5+.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-facetwp-modules-group-selection.png)Available FacetWP modules in the Beaver Builder add-on v1.5+.
Since v1.5, the FacetWP Beaver Builder add-on comes with three built-in modules:

1. [Facet](#the-facetwp-facet-module) – adds a [facet](/help-center/facets/).
2. [Listing](#the-facetwp-listing-module) – adds a [Listing Builder listing](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/).
3. [Button](#the-facetwp-button-module) – adds an [Apply button](#add-an-apply-button), a [Flyout button](#add-a-flyout-button) or a [Submit button](#add-a-submit-button).

To quickly find these modules in the module selector, filter by “FacetWP Modules” in the Group dropdown.

Note: In versions **older than v1.5** of the add-on, there were *seven* FacetWP modules. The Template module is now named Listing module. The Pager, Per Page, Counts, Selections and Sort modules are now **deprecated** and will be **non-functional after updating**. We recommend [migrating them to the updated Facet module](#migrate-deprecated-facetwp-modules), which replaces them all. If you want to keep using the old modules, it is possible to [re-enable them with a hook](#re-enable-deprecated-facetwp-modules).

### The FacetWP Facet module

![The FacetWP Facet module settings.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-facetwp-facet-module.png)The FacetWP Facet module settings.
With a “Facet” module, you can add an [existing facet](/help-center/facets/) to your page. It is the recommended alternative to [adding facet shortcodes with an “HTML” module](#add-facetwp-shortcodes-with-an-html-module).

After dragging the Facet module to your page, use the “Facet” setting dropdown to select one of your existing facets.

The dropdown will show all previously created facets. At the bottom, you can select the [User Selections facet](/help-center/facets/facet-types/user-selections/) (which does not need to be created, and does not appear in the facet overview screen of FacetWP’s settings).

Optionally, add a facet heading by entering a text in the “Heading” field and selecting the HTML tag (
```
<h1>
```

 – 
```
<h6>
```

) for it in the “Heading tag” field.

Most facet types will render in the Beaver Builder editor, after saving their settings. Before saving, and for some facet types, a placeholder with the facet label is shown:

![The FacetWP Facet module placeholder, with a heading text set in the 'Heading' setting.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-facetwp-facet-module-placeholder.png)The FacetWP Facet module placeholder, with a heading text set in the “Heading” setting.
![FacetWP modules in the sidebar Outline.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-facetwp-modules-outline.png)FacetWP modules and their (customizable) labels displayed in the sidebar Outline.
All FacetWP modules will automatically display their (customizable) labels in the sidebar Outline, after saving.

Facets will display but not actually function in the Beaver Builder editor. Check your front-end page to see if your facets are functioning correctly. If they are not appearing, make sure you have also added a listing to the page. This can be a [supported FacetWP-enabled Beaver Builder module](#supported-modules), a [Listing Builder listing](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/) (added with a [Listing module](#the-facetwp-listing-module)), or any [other supported listing template type](/help-center/listing-templates/).

If you want to hide empty facets (facets with no choices left after filtering), including their heading, you can use [this code snippet](/help-center/facets/#hide-empty-facets).

Note:To translate the FacetWP Facet module’s Heading text when using WPML, [see the explanation below](#translate-facet-module-headings-and-button-module-labels-in-wpml).

### The FacetWP Listing module

![The FacetWP Listing module settings.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-facetwp-listing-module.png)The FacetWP Listing module settings.
With a “Listing” module you can add an existing [Listing Builder listing template](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/) to your page. It is the recommended alternative to [adding listing shortcodes with an “HTML” module](#add-facetwp-shortcodes-with-an-html-module).

After dragging the Listing module to your page, use the “Listing” setting dropdown to select one of your existing [Listing Builder listings](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/). The dropdown will show all previously created listings.

The Listing module will render your listing, including its full layout, in the Beaver Builder editor. (Most) facet types will render, but not actually function. Check your front-end page to see if your facets are correctly filtering your listing.

All Listing modules will automatically display their (customizable) label in the sidebar Outline, after saving.

Important:There should be **only one filterable (non-static) FacetWP listing module** on a page. FacetWP does **not** support multiple filterable Listing Builder listings on a page. If you do add more than one on a page, even though they will render the Beaver Builder editor, they will **not work** in the front-end. If you want to use multiple listings, the only way is to [add the extra ones as  “static” (non-filterable) listings](#add-a-static-listing).

#### Use a FacetWP Listing module on a WP archive template

Generally, on archive templates, it would be better **not** to use a Listing module, because by default, FacetWP uses the archive’s query, not the Listing module’s custom query. Using a Listing module in this case will cause a mismatch between your facets and the posts displayed in the listing.

The recommended setup is to choose [another supported module](#supported-modules), with its [query source set to use the “Main Query”](#using-modules-on-archive-templates).

However, if you don’t want or cannot change the way things are set up, and want to keep using a Listing module on a WP archive template, you have to tell FacetWP explicitly which query (not) to use. Two ways to do this [are described here](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/#using-a-listing-builder-listing-template-on-a-wp-archive) (option 1 and 2).

#### Add a static listing

![How to add a static listing with the FacetWP Listing module.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-facetwp-listing-module-static.png)How to add a static listing with the FacetWP Listing module.
Using the “Static” setting, you can add a [static Listing Builder listing](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/#multiple-listing-templates-on-the-same-page) with the FacetWP Listing widget. A “static” listing is a listing that does not react to facets.

You can even add **multiple static listings** to a page, also to a page that already has a “normal” filterable (non-static) listing. But there should be **only one filterable (non-static) listing on a page**.

Note that static Listing Builder listings [can also be added with a shortcode](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/#multiple-listing-templates-on-the-same-page). When using a shortcode, you can [add them with an “HTML” module](#add-facetwp-shortcodes-with-an-html-module).

### The FacetWP Button module

![The FacetWP Button module settings.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-facetwp-button-module.png)The FacetWP Button module settings.
With a “Button” module, you can add three types of buttons to your page:

1. An [Apply button](#add-an-apply-button) – disables auto-refresh and applies facet selections on button click.
2. A [Flyout button](#add-a-flyout-button) – opens the Mobile Flyout panel. This option is only available with the [Mobile Flyout add-on](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/mobile-flyout/) installed.
3. A [Submit button](#add-a-submit-button) – redirects facet selections to a separate results page. This option is only available with the [Submit button add-on](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/submit-button/) installed.

After dragging the Button module to your page, use the “Button type” setting to select the desired button type. Then change the button text, icon, and other styling settings as needed.

FacetWP button modules will automatically display their button type in the sidebar Outline, after saving.

Note:To translate the FacetWP Button module’s button text when using WPML, [see the explanation below](#translate-facet-module-headings-and-button-module-labels-in-wpml).

#### Add an “Apply” button

![Add an 'Apply' button with a Beaver Builder FacetWP Button module.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/bb-facetwp-button-module-apply.png)Add an “Apply” button with a Beaver Builder FacetWP Button module.
When you add an “**Apply**” button to your page, it will disable FacetWP’s auto-refresh function and apply facet selections only when the button is clicked.

Adding this button type does exactly the same as explained in our tutorial on [how to disable facet auto-refresh and add an apply/submit button](/how-to-disable-facet-auto-refresh-and-add-an-apply-button/). It will also automatically add [the mentioned JavaScript snippet that disables auto-refresh](/how-to-disable-facet-auto-refresh-and-add-an-apply-button/#disable-auto-refresh) to your page.  As described in this tutorial, this button will disable auto-refresh for all [facet types](/help-center/facets/facet-types/), except for [Pager facets](/help-center/facets/facet-types/pager/) and [Reset facets](/help-center/facets/facet-types/reset/). If needed, you can re-enable auto-refresh for individual facets or facet types with 
```
FWP.refresh()
```

, for example [when using a Sort facet](/how-to-disable-facet-auto-refresh-and-add-an-apply-button/#re-enable-auto-refresh-for-sort-facets).

#### Add a “Flyout” button

![Add a flyout button with a Beaver Builder FacetWP Button module.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/bb-facetwp-button-module-flyout.png)Add a flyout button with a Beaver Builder FacetWP Button module.
To be able to create a Flyout button, first install the [Mobile Flyout add-on](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/mobile-flyout/). When you then add a “**Flyout**” button module to your page, the button will open the Flyout panel, [as described here](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/mobile-flyout/#usage).

#### Add a “Submit” button

![The FacetWP Button module Listing page URL redirect setting.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-facetwp-submit-button-module.png)The FacetWP Button module Listing page URL redirect setting.
To be able to create a Submit button, first install the [Submit button add-on](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/submit-button/).

The Submit Button module is a convenient alternative to adding the Submit button manually or with a shortcode, [as described in the Submit button add-on’s setup instructions](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/submit-button/#how-to-set-it-up).

After following the setup instructions and adding the Submit Button module, clicking the button will redirect the page to the URL specified in the “Listing page” setting field of the module. When facets on the page have selections, they will be included in the redirect.

Make sure to enter a full URL (starting with 
```
https://
```

) in the “Listing page” setting field, or at least start it with a forward slash, e.g. 
```
/my-result-page/
```

.

Note:To translate the FacetWP Button module’s button text and the Submit button’s listing page URL when using WPML, [see the explanation below](#translate-facet-module-headings-and-button-module-labels-in-wpml).

## Add FacetWP shortcodes with an HTML module

![How to use an HTML module to add facets or listing shortcodes.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-facet-shorcode-html-module.png)How to use an HTML module to add facets or listing shortcodes.
Instead of [using FacetWP modules](#facetwp-modules) (which is recommended), [facets](/help-center/facets/), [listing templates](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/) and [other features](/help-center/developers/shortcodes-reference/) can also be added with their shortcode in Beaver Builder HTML module.

Just copy the shortcode from the FacetWP overview page or facet/listing settings page, and paste it into the module.

## Supported pagination

In most scenarios, FacetWP will automatically detect **numbered** pagination of [supported modules](#supported-modules), and make it work with FacetWP’s AJAX refresh.

You can also set the module’s pagination to “None”, create a [Pager facet](/help-center/facets/facet-types/pager/) instead, and [add it with a Facet module](#the-facetwp-facet-module). The Pager facet also has a “Load more” pager type.

Important:FacetWP does ﻿**not** support any “Scroll” and “Load more” type pagination that some of the supported modules offer in their settings. Use a [Pager facet](/help-center/facets/facet-types/pager/) instead, which includes a “Load more” option, and [add it with a Facet module](#the-facetwp-facet-module).

## Using modules on archive templates

![Use 'Main Query' the Source setting of modules in archive templates.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-loop-module-source-setting-main-query.png)Use “Main Query” the Source setting of modules in archive templates.
Beaver Themer allows you to design layouts for archive pages (blog posts archive, taxonomy archives, etc).

If you add a [supported module](#supported-modules) to an archive template, make sure to set its Source setting to “**Main Query**“. This ensures the WordPress archive query is used for both Beaver Builder and FacetWP.

Important: Using “Main Query” as Source in archive templates with FacetWP enabled is **required**. Don’t use “Custom Query” in archive templates, as this will make FacetWP detect and use the wrong query for filtering, causing a mismatch between your facets and the posts displayed in the listing. If you intend to customize your query arguments (like 
```
posts_per_page
```

), check the tutorial on [how to customize WordPress archive queries.](https://facetwp.com/how-to-customize-wp-archive-queries/)
Using a [Listing module](#the-facetwp-listing-module) in an archive template is not recommended, because it creates a custom query. If you do this, make sure to read [this section about how to make sure FacetWP is using the right query](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/#using-a-listing-builder-listing-template-on-a-wp-archive).

Note that in archive templates, facets may work even without enabling the “FacetWP” setting, because of FacetWP’s query autodetection. However, to prevent issues with pagination, make sure to always enable the “FacetWP” setting, also in archive templates.

### Using a FacetWP Listing module on a WP archive template

This is not recommended. See [the explanation above](#use-a-facetwp-listing-module-on-a-wp-archive-template).

## Customize a module’s query arguments

If you need to adapt or override the query arguments of a [supported module](#supported-modules) (except FacetWP’s [Listing module](#customize-a-listing-modules-query-arguments)), you can use Beaver Builder’s 
```
fl_builder_loop_query_args
```

 hook. For example, if you want to display only specific custom post types, change the order, or use a meta query.

This hook works similar to WP’s [pre_get_posts hook](/how-to-customize-wp-archive-queries/#how-to-use-pre_get_posts-to-modify-the-wp-archive-query). It exposes the [WP_Query](https://developer.wordpress.org/reference/classes/wp_query/) object and allows you to customize the query in any way you want.

The following example adds a fallback sorting method by overriding the original 
```
orderby
```

 query argument, on the front page only:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More infofunction my_query_order( $query_args ) {
  if ( is_front_page() ) {
    $query_args['orderby'] = array(
      'date'  => 'DESC', // Primary sort: by post date
      'title' => 'ASC'   // Secondary, fallback sort: by post title
    );
  }
  return $query_args;
}
add_filter( 'fl_builder_loop_query_args', 'my_query_order' );
```

Note:This specific example of adding a fallback sorting method can be useful if you are trying to [fix duplicate results in your listing](/how-to-prevent-duplicate-results/).
For more examples of how to use this hook, including how to apply it only to a specific module, see [this Beaver Builder tutorial](https://docs.wpbeaverbuilder.com/beaver-builder/2.9/developer/tutorials-guides/create-a-filter-to-customize-the-display-of-post-data).

For an overview of all available Beaver Builder hooks, see [this page](https://hooks.wpbeaverbuilder.com/bb-plugin/).

Warning:The 
```
fl_builder_loop_query_args
```

 hook only works for modules that have their “Source” setting set to “**Custom Query**“. If you are [using your module in an archive template](#using-modules-on-archive-templates), with its “Source” setting set to “**Main Query**“, check the tutorial on [how to customize WordPress archive queries](https://facetwp.com/how-to-customize-wp-archive-queries/), with the 
```
pre_get_posts
```

 hook.

### Customize a Listing module’s query arguments

To change the query arguments of a [Listing module](#the-facetwp-listing-module), you can just change its settings. Or, if you need more flexibility, use the listing’s [Dev mode](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/using-the-listing-builder-in-dev-mode/). Or you can use the [facetwp_query_args hook](/help-center/developers/hooks/querying-hooks/facetwp_query_args/).

## Prevent unwanted scrolling

Beaver Builder, by itself, automatically scrolls the page to the top of the module when the page loads.

On pages with FacetWP-enabled modules, you will normally **not** see this scroll, because the JavaScript in FacetWP’s Beaver Builder add-on prevents it from happening. So if you *do* see the page scrolling when it loads, something is wrong.

When you open your browser Console on the page with the scroll, you’ll likely see one of the following errors, or something similar referencing 
```
FLBuilderLayout
```

:

```
Uncaught ReferenceError: FLBuilderLayout is not defined
-or-
ReferenceError: Can’t find variable: FLBuilderLayout
```

This error is caused by Beaver Builder rendering its JavaScript assets inline in the footer, **after** FacetWP’s Beaver Builder add-on 
```
front.js
```

 script that prevents the scrolling. FacetWP’s script expects Beaver Builder’s JavaScript to load **before** it.

There are three possible causes of Beaver Buildering rendering its JavaScript inline in the footer:

1. ![To fix the JavaScript error and page scroll, disable Beaver Builder's 'Render CSS/JS assets inline' setting in Settings > Beaver Builder > Advanced > Frontend.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/03/beaverbuilder_render_css_js_assets_inline.png)To fix the JavaScript error and page scroll, disable Beaver Builder’s “Render CSS/JS assets inline” setting in Settings > Beaver Builder > Advanced > Frontend.
If the “Render CSS/JS assets inline” setting is enabled. This setting can be found in Settings > Beaver Builder > Advanced > Frontend. Disable this setting to fix the error and the page scroll.
2. If you are using Beaver Builder’s [fl_builder_render_assets_inline](https://docs.wpbeaverbuilder.com/beaver-builder/developer/how-to-tips/load-css-and-javascript-inline/) filter in your (child) theme’s functions.php, or in a code snippets plugin. To fix the error and page scroll, just remove this filter:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More info// DON'T use this. REMOVE it if you have this in your functions.php
add_filter( 'fl_builder_render_assets_inline', '__return_true' );
```

1. The same situation can also be caused by a caching/optimization plugin. To fix the issue, check its settings and make sure to not load JavaScript deferred, and don’t combine scripts. To fix the issue, FacetWP’s Beaver Builder add-on’s 
```
front.js
```

 script needs to load **after** all Beaver Builder scripts.

### Prevent PowerPack Content Grid module scrolling

If you are using any [PowerPack Content Grid modules](https://wpbeaveraddons.com/demo/content-grid/) on a page that also has (any type of) FacetWP-enabled post listing, depending on your setup, you may see unexpected automatic scrolling to the top of these PowerPack modules when the page is (pre-)loaded with a facet selection. To prevent these automatic scrolls, add the following code to your (child) theme’s functions.php:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More infoadd_action( 'pp_cg_before_posts', function( $settings, $query ) {
  $paged = $GLOBALS['wp_the_query']->get( 'paged' );
  $paged = ( $paged < 2 ) ? 0 : $paged;
  $GLOBALS['wp_the_query']->set( 'page', $paged );
  $GLOBALS['wp_the_query']->set( 'paged', $paged );
}, 10, 2 );
```

## Deprecated FacetWP modules and features

In v1.5 of the add-on, two things were deprecated:

- [A range of older FacetWP modules](#migrate-deprecated-facetwp-modules)
- [Selecting FacetWP Listing Builder listing templates in supported modules, other than the Listing module](#migrate-facetwp-listing-templates-in-the-source-dropdown)

### Migrate deprecated FacetWP modules

![FacetWP modules in add-on versions older than v1.5.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-deprecated-modules.png)FacetWP modules in add-on versions older than v1.5.
In pre-v1.5 versions of the add-on, there were seven FacetWP modules, as shown on the right.

In v1.5, the Facet module was updated, and the Template module was renamed to Listing module. The other five modules are now **deprecated**. Their functionality can now be added with a [Facet module](#the-facetwp-facet-module).

![Deprecated FacetWP modules in the sidebar Outline, after updating to v1.5+.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-deprecated-modules-outline-without-hook.png)Deprecated FacetWP modules in the sidebar Outline, after updating to v1.5+.
After updating the add-on to v1.5+, any pre-existing old modules will display in the sidebar Outline as deprecated modules, like in the image on the right. They will be non-functional, with no way to change any settings or add new ones.

It is possible to [re-enable these depreccated modules](#re-enable-deprecated-facetwp-modules), but we **highly recommend** migrating them to the new modules, as outlined in the table below:

| Old module (pre v1.5) | New situation in v1.5+ |
| --- | --- |
| Facet | Still exists as the new Facet module, which supports all facet types, also Pager, Sort, and User Selections. |
| Template | Still exists and was renamed to the new Listing module, which supports all listings and can also be used to add static listings. |
| Pager | Use the Facet module instead to add a Pager facet of type “Page numbers“. |
| Per Page | Use the Facet module instead to add a Pager facet of type “Per page“. |
| Counts | Use the Facet module instead to add a Pager facet of type “Result counts“. |
| Sort | Use the Facet module instead to add a Sort facet. |
| Selections | Use the Facet module instead to add a User Selections facet. You can find the User Selections facet at the bottom of the facet selection dropdown in the module settings. |

#### Find all used deprecated modules to migrate

To find all deprecated modules to migrate in all your posts, pages, and templates, you can (temporarily) add the 
```
facetwp_bb_enable_deprecated
```

 hook to your (child) theme’s functions.php:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More info// Re-enable deprecated FacetWP modules, and
// re-enable FacetWP listings in some module's "Source" settings dropdown
add_filter( 'facetwp_bb_enable_deprecated', '__return_true' );
```

This hook [re-enables the deprecated FacetWP modules](#re-enable-deprecated-facetwp-modules), which also lets them show up in [Beaver Builder’s Modules tab](https://docs.wpbeaverbuilder.com/beaver-builder/2.9/settings/modules), found in Settings > Beaver Builder > Modules. This makes it easier to find them all.

![Show deprecated FacetWP modules with the 'facetwp_bb_enable_deprecated' hook, and the number of times used with the 'is_module_disable_enabled hook' in Beaver Builder's Modules tab.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-enabled-modules-settings.png)Show deprecated FacetWP modules with the 
```
facetwp_bb_enable_deprecated
```

 hook, and the number of times used with the 
```
is_module_disable_enabled
```

 hook, in Beaver Builder’s Modules tab (found under Settings > Beaver Builder > Modules).
To also see the number of times a module is used, on how many posts, pages, and templates, add the 
```
is_module_disable_enabled
```

 hook, as shown below.

The Modules tab will then look like the image on the right.

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More info// Re-enable deprecated FacetWP modules, and
// re-enable FacetWP listings in some module's "Source" settings dropdown
add_filter( 'facetwp_bb_enable_deprecated', '__return_true' );

// Show the number of times each module used and on how many pages/posts/templates, in Settings > Beaver Builder > Modules
add_filter( 'is_module_disable_enabled', '__return_true' );
```

After you finish migrating, you can **remove both hooks**.

### Re-enable deprecated FacetWP modules

We recommend migrating all deprecated modules to the new modules, as outlined in [the table above](#migrate-deprecated-facetwp-modules).

However, if you have reasons to want to keep using these old modules, you can re-enable them by adding the following hook to your (child) theme’s functions.php:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More info// Re-enable deprecated FacetWP modules, and
// re-enable FacetWP listings in some module's "Source" settings dropdown
add_filter( 'facetwp_bb_enable_deprecated', '__return_true' );
```

![Re-enabled deprecated FacetWP modules in the sidebar Outline, after updating to v1.5+ and with the facetwp_bb_enable_deprecated hook.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-deprecated-modules-outline-with-hook.png)Re-enabled deprecated FacetWP modules in the sidebar Outline, after updating to v1.5+ and with the 
```
facetwp_bb_enable_deprecated
```

 hook.
![Re-enabled deprecated FacetWP modules in the Module selector.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-deprecated-modules-selector.png)Re-enabled deprecated FacetWP modules in the Module selector.
After adding this hook, the deprecated modules will look like the images on the right. They will keep a “Deprecated” label, but will be fully functional again.

In each module’s settings, you’ll also see a deprecation message with instructions for [migrating](#migrate-deprecated-facetwp-modules).

The deprecated modules will appear in a separate tab in the “FacetWP Modules” group in the Module selector, and you can add new instances of them.

### Migrate FacetWP listing templates in the Source dropdown

![Using a Listing Builder listing template in the Source setting in a supported Beaver Builder listing module (deprecated).](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-deprecated-listings-as-source.png)Using a Listing Builder listing template in the Source setting in a supported Beaver Builder listing module (deprecated).
In add-on versions **older than v1.5**, the following [supported modules](#supported-modules) will display all available FacetWP [Listing Builder listing templates](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/) in their settings’ “Source” dropdown:

- Posts
- Advanced Posts (UABB)
- Woo – Products (UABB) – only product-based listing templates will work
- Content Grid (PowerPack)
- Product Grid (PowerPack/WooPack) – only product-based listing templates will work

If you choose one of your listing templates as source, that listing template’s query arguments will be used in the module (not its layout or styling).

In version v1.5+ of the add-on, using FacetWP listing templates as module source is **deprecated**. You will now only see listing templates in the Source dropdown if one was already selected before updating to v1.5+, or if you [re-enabled this feature](#re-enable-facetwp-listing-templates-in-the-source-dropdown).

We recommend selecting “Custom Query” in the dropdown and recreating your listing template’s query arguments. Or, if you want to display a listing template, including its layout and styling, switch to a [Listing module](#the-facetwp-listing-module).

#### Re-enable FacetWP listing templates in the Source dropdown

If you need to re-enable the display of FacetWP listing templates in the Source dropdown, add the 
```
facetwp_bb_enable_deprecated
```

 hook to your (child) theme’s functions.php.

If you use the hook without any arguments, it will also [re-enable deprecated FacetWP modules](#re-enable-deprecated-facetwp-modules):

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More info// Re-enable deprecated FacetWP modules, and
// re-enable FacetWP listings in some module's "Source" settings dropdown
add_filter( 'facetwp_bb_enable_deprecated', '__return_true' );
```

If you want to **only** re-enable FacetWP listing templates in the “Source” dropdown, use the hook like this:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More info// Only re-enable FacetWP listings in some module's "Source" settings dropdown
add_filter( 'facetwp_bb_enable_deprecated', function( $enabled, $type) {
  if ( 'query_options' == $type ) {
    return true;
  }
  return $enabled;
}, 10, 2 );
```

After using one of these two hook, listings will show up again in the dropdown, but with a “Deprecated” label.

Note that if a Listing Builder listing template is selected in the “Source” dropdown, a deprecation message is shown in the module settings, as can be seen in [above image](#migrate-facetwp-listing-templates-in-the-source-dropdown).

## Known issues, incompatibilities, and unsupported module settings

There are a few things known to not work with this add-on:

### WooCommerce module

- ![Only Layout > Multiple Products is supported in a WooCommerce module. For the Products Source child setting, all options are supported.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/bb-woocommerce-multiple-products.png)Only Layout > Multiple Products is supported in a WooCommerce module. For the Products Source child setting, all options are supported.
The WooCommere module **only** works with the setting: Layout > Multiple Products. For the Products Source child setting, all options are supported.

### Fix gaps in the WooCommerce module layout after using a Load More Pager facet

If you are using a Beaver Builder WooCommerce module, you’ll notice gaps in the layout after using a [Load more type Pager facet](/help-center/facets/facet-types/pager/#load-more-pager-type). These gaps are caused by the module’s CSS, which sets 
```
float
```

, 
```
clear
```

, and 
```
margin-right
```

 rules based on classes added to the first and last item in the retrieved posts.

To fix these gaps, you can use one of the solutions below.

The first approach is to fix the wrong clearing and right margins. The following example is for a 4-column layout. Adapt 
```
4n
```

 in [line 20](#fix-woo-commerce-module-gaps-floats-L20) and [line 25](#fix-woo-commerce-module-gaps-floats-L25) for other numbers of columns. The code only applies to 
```
min-width: 768px
```

, like the default CSS does. Add other media queries for other widths if needed.

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More infoadd_action('facetwp_scripts', function () {
  ?>
  <style>
    @media (min-width: 768px) {

      /* Reset some of the default styles */

      .woocommerce[class*="columns-"] ul.products li.product  {
        clear: none !important;
      }

      .woocommerce[class*="columns-"] ul.products li.product.last {
        clear: none !important;
        margin-right: 3.8% !important;
      }

      /* New styles for 4 columns  */

      /* Clear every 5th item */
      .woocommerce[class*="columns-"] ul.products li.product:nth-child(4n+1)  {
        clear: both !important;
      }

      /* Remove right margin for every 4th item */
      .woocommerce[class*="columns-"] ul.products li.product:nth-child(4n)  {
        margin-right: 0 !important;
      }

    }
  </style>
  <?php
}, 100);
```

The second approach is to remove the floating behavior and let the layout use CSS grid.

In this example we apply a 4-column grid. Adapt the number in [line 22](#fix-woo-commerce-module-gaps-grid-L22) for other numbers of columns. The code only applies to 
```
min-width: 768px
```

, like the default CSS does. Add other media queries for other widths if needed.

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More infoadd_action('facetwp_scripts', function () {
  ?>
  <style>
    @media (min-width: 768px) {

      /* Reset some of the default styles */

      .woocommerce[class*="columns-"] ul.products:before  {
        display: none !important;
      }

      .woocommerce[class*="columns-"] ul.products li.product  {
        margin-right: 0 !important;
        float: none !important;
      }

      /* New styles for 4 columns with CSS grid */

      .woocommerce[class*="columns-"] ul.products  {
        display: grid !important;
        grid-template-columns: repeat(4, 1fr); /* 4 columns */
        grid-gap: 3.8%; /* Adapt as needed */
      }

      .woocommerce[class*="columns-"] ul.products[class*="columns-"] li.product  {
        width: auto !important;
      }

    }
  </style>
  <?php
}, 100);
```

### PowerPack Content Grid module

- The Content Grid module is only supported with the setting: Content > Layout > Grid. Carousel layout is **not** supported.
- FacetWP does **not** work with [Taxonomy Filters](https://wpbeaveraddons.com/docs/powerpack/modules/content-grid/how-to-enable-taxonomy-filters-in-content-grid/), located under Content > Content Settings > Post Taxonomy Filter > Enable Post Filter. To filter taxonomies, use [facets](/help-center/facets/) added in a [Facet module](#the-facetwp-facet-module) instead.

### UABB Advanced Posts module

- The UABB Advanced Posts module does **not** work with a [Load More Pager facet](/help-center/facets/facet-types/pager/#load-more-pager-type) when the Post Appearance setting is set to **Carousel layout**.
- The Advanced Posts module does **not** work with the Taxonomy Filter setting that can be set under the Query tab for Grid and Masonry layouts (set under Post Appearance). This feature adds AJAX filters that do **not** work together with FacetWP.

### UABB Woo Products module

- The UABB Woo Products module does **not** work with a [Load More Pager facet](/help-center/facets/facet-types/pager/#load-more-pager-type) when the Layout setting is set to **Carousel layout**.

### Scroll- or Load More type pagination in all modules

- FacetWP does ﻿**not** support any “Scroll” and “Load more” type pagination that some of the supported modules offer in their settings. Use a [Pager facet](/help-center/facets/facet-types/pager/) instead, which includes a “Load more” option, and [add it with a Facet module](#the-facetwp-facet-module).

## Translate Facet module headings and Button module labels in WPML

![The WPML Translation Editor page setting.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/wpml_translation_editor_page_setting.png)The “WPML Translation Editor” page setting.
If you are using FacetWP with the Beaver Builder add-on v1.5+, WPML, and [the Multilingual add-on](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/multilingual/), and you are translating your page(s) with the [WPML Advanced Translation Editor](https://wpml.org/documentation/translating-your-contents/advanced-translation-editor/) (chosen with the middle option in the “Translation this Document” page setting — as shown in the image on the right), the following items will show up in the Translation Editor to be translated:

- Heading texts set in any [FacetWP Facet module](#the-facetwp-facet-module) on the page.
- Button text labels set in any [FacetWP Button module](#the-facetwp-button-module) on the page.
- Listing page URLs set in any FacetWP Button module on the page that is [set to the “Submit button” button type](#add-a-submit-button).

These translatable items will look like this:

![Translatable text/labels of FacetWP Beaver Builder modules in the WPML Advanced Translation Editor.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/wpml_advanced_translation_editor_beaver_builder_modules.png)Translatable text/labels of FacetWP Beaver Builder modules in the WPML Advanced Translation Editor.
The alternative is to translate these items in the Beaver Builder editor of the translated page itself (with the “Translate this Document” page setting set to “WordPress Editor” (the right option)).

Note:If you are using the [Mobile Flyout add-on](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/mobile-flyout/), the facet headings/labels that [are automatically created](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/mobile-flyout/#facet-headings-labels) can be translated with the [facetwp_i18n hook](/help-center/developers/hooks/advanced-hooks/facetwp_i18n/).
