## Elementor

**Source:** [https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/elementor/](https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/elementor/)

This add-on lets you display facets alongside [a range of Elementor listing widgets](#supported-widgets). These widgets are available when using [Elementor Pro](https://elementor.com/pro), or one of the mentioned Elementor plugins.

With this add-on installed, FacetWP also adds [three widgets of its own](#facetwp-elementor-widgets), for adding facets, [Listing Builder listings](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/), and FacetWP-related buttons. These widgets also work with the free version of Elementor.

Warning:In version 3.22.0, Elementor introduced a new [Element Caching](https://elementor.com/help/element-caching-help/) feature. **Element Caching is incompatible with FacetWP**. If your facets are not showing (or disappearing after use), or if you see a “FacetWP was unable to auto-detect the post listing” error, make sure to [disable Element Caching globally or in every FacetWP-related widget](#fix-issues-caused-by-elementors-element-caching-feature).

## Supported widgets

The following widgets are supported and can be used to [create FacetWP compatible listings](#usage). Note that all widgets [need to be enabled for FacetWP](#usage) to be filterable by facets.

| Plugin | Widget name |
| --- | --- |
| Elementor Pro | Loop Grid
Loop Carousel
Posts
Archive Posts
Products (with WooCommerce)
Archive Products (with WooCommerce) |
| Ultimate Addons for Elementor | Posts
Woo – Products (with WooCommerce) |
| Premium Addons for Elementor | Blog
Woo – Products (with WooCommerce) |
| PowerPack for Elementor | Advanced Posts
Woo – Products (with WooCommerce) |
| JetEngine | Listing Grid |
| AnyWhere Elementor Pro | Post Blocks
Post Blocks Adv
FacetWP does not support AEP directly. The integration is built into AEP. 
Note: to disable the scroll on page load, see the instructions below. |

The FacetWP Elementor add-on adds the following widgets:

| Plugin | Widget name |
| --- | --- |
| FacetWP | FacetWP Facet
FacetWP Listing
FacetWP Button |

## Usage

![How to enable FacetWP in a supported Elementor listing widget.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/03/elementor-enable-facetwp.png)How to enable FacetWP in a supported Elementor listing widget.
After installation and activation, there is no further configuration needed for this add-on plugin.

When it is active, [supported Elementor listing widgets](#supported-widgets) will display an extra “Enable FacetWP” setting. If this setting is enabled, FacetWP will use this listing as the one to dynamically filter with facets.

The Archive Posts and WooCommerce Archive Products widgets [are meant to be used on WP archive pages](#using-facetwp-on-wp-archive-templates). Other widgets, like the Posts widget or the Query Loop widget, can be used both on normal pages and on WP archive templates ([if you set the Query Source setting to use the “Current Query”](#using-a-posts-loop-grid-woo-products-or-blog-widget-on-a-wp-archive-template)).

Important:Make sure there is only **one FacetWP-enabled listing widget on a page**. Using multiple FacetWP-enabled listing widgets per page is **not** supported.
After enabling your widget, add one or more [facets](/help-center/facets/) to the page. You can use facet shortcodes in a Shortcode widget, or use the FacetWP Facet widget. The Elementor add-on adds widgets to add facets, listings, and FacetWP-related buttons:

## FacetWP Elementor widgets

![Built-in FacetWP Elementor widgets.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/08/facetwp-elementor-widgets.png)Built-in FacetWP Elementor widgets.
Since v1.8, the FacetWP Elementor add-on comes with three built-in Elementor widgets:

1. [FacetWP Facet](#the-facetwp-facet-widget) – adds a [facet](/help-center/facets/).
2. [FacetWP Listing](#the-facetwp-listing-widget) – adds a [Listing Builder listing](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/).
3. [FacetWP Button](#the-facetwp-button-widget) – adds an [Apply button](#add-an-apply-button), a [Flyout button](#add-a-flyout-button) or a [Submit button](#add-a-submit-button).

### The FacetWP Facet widget

![The FacetWP Elementor Facet widget settings.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/facetwp-elementor-addon-facet-widget-settings.png)The FacetWP Elementor Facet widget settings.
With a “FacetWP Facet” widget you can add an [existing facet](/help-center/facets/) to your Elementor page. It is the recommended alternative to [adding facet shortcodes with a “Shortcode” or “HTML” widget](#add-facetwp-shortcodes-with-a-shortcode-widget).

After adding the widget to your page, use the “Facet” setting dropdown to select one of your existing facets.

The dropdown will show all previously created facets, including the [User Selections facet](/help-center/facets/facet-types/user-selections/) (which does not appear in the facet overview screen of FacetWP’s settings).

Optionally, add a facet heading by entering a text in the “Header” field and selecting the HTML tag to use for it. If you want to hide empty facets (facets with no choices left), including their heading, you can use [this code snippet](/help-center/facets/#hide-empty-facets).

![The FacetWP Elementor Facet widget editor placeholder.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/08/facetwp-elementor-addon-facet-widget-placeholder.png)The FacetWP Elementor Facet widget editor placeholder.
Note that facets will **not** display in the Elementor editor: a placeholder is shown instead. Check your front-end page to see if your facets are functioning correctly. If they are not appearing, make sure you also have added a listing to the page. This can be a [supported Elementor widget](#supported-widgets), a [Listing Builder listing](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/) (added with a [Listing widget](#the-facetwp-listing-widget)), or any [other supported listing template type](/help-center/listing-templates/).

Note:The FacetWP Facet widget’s Header text is translatable [with the facetwp_18n hook](/help-center/developers/hooks/advanced-hooks/facetwp_i18n/). When using WPML, with [the Multilingual add-on](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/multilingual/) installed, the Header text is also translatable with WPML’s Advanced Translation Editor, [as explained below](#translate-facet-widget-headings-and-button-widget-labels-in-wpml).

### The FacetWP Listing widget

![The FacetWP Elementor Listing widget settings.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/facetwp-elementor-add-on-listing-widget-settings.png)The FacetWP Elementor Listing widget settings.
With a “FacetWP Listing” widget you can add an existing [Listing Builder listing template](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/) to your Elementor page. It is the recommended alternative to [adding listing shortcodes with a “Shortcode” or “HTML” widget](#add-facetwp-shortcodes-with-a-shortcode-widget).

After adding the widget to your page, use the “Listing” setting dropdown to select one of your previously created [Listing Builder listings](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/).

[Unlike the Facet widget](#the-facetwp-facet-widget), the Listing widget will show your listing, including its full layout, in the Elementor editor.

Important:There should be **only one filterable (non-static) FacetWP listing widget** on a page. FacetWP does **not** support multiple filterable Listing Builder listings on a page. If you do add more than one on a page, even though they will appear to work in the Elementor edit screen, they will **not work** in the front-end. If you want to use multiple listings, the only way is to [add them as  “static” (non-filterable) listings](#add-a-static-listing).

#### Use a FacetWP Listing widget on a WP archive template

Generally, on archive templates, it would be better **not** to use a Listing widget, because by default, FacetWP uses the archive’s query, not the Listing widget’s custom query. Using a Listing widget in this case will cause a mismatch between your facets and the posts displayed in the listing.

The recommended setup is to choose a widget that uses the template’s native archive query, like an [Archive Posts or Archive Products widget](#using-facetwp-on-wp-archive-templates), or a widget that can have its query set to use the “Current Query”, [like a Posts, Loop Grid, or Woo Products widget](#using-a-posts-loop-grid-woo-products-or-blog-widget-on-a-wp-archive-template).

However, if you don’t want or cannot change the way things are set up, and want to keep using a Listing widget on a WP archive template, you have to tell FacetWP explicitly which query (not) to use. Two ways to do this [are described here](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/#using-a-listing-builder-listing-template-on-a-wp-archive) (option 1 and 2).

#### Add a static listing

![Add a static listing with the FacetWP Elementor Listing widget.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/facetwp-elementor-add-on-static-listing-widget-settings.png)Add a static listing with the FacetWP Elementor Listing widget.
Since version 1.9.3 of the add-on, it is possible to add [static Listing Builder listings](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/#multiple-listing-templates-on-the-same-page) with the FacetWP Listing widget. A “static” listing is a listing that does not react to facets.

You can even add **multiple static listings** to a page, also to a page that already has a “normal” filterable (non-static) listing. But there should be **only one filterable (non-static) listing on a page**.

Note that static Listing Builder listings [can also be added with a shortcode or PHP](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/#multiple-listing-templates-on-the-same-page). When using a shortcode, you can add them  [with a Shortcode widget](#add-facetwp-shortcodes-with-a-shortcode-widget).

### The FacetWP Button widget

![The FacetWP Elementor Button widget settings.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/facetwp-elementor-addon-button-widget-settings.png)The FacetWP Elementor Button widget settings.
With a “FacetWP Button” widget you can add three types of buttons to your Elementor page:

1. An [Apply button](#add-an-apply-button) – disables auto-refresh and applies facet selections on button click.
2. A [Flyout button](#add-a-flyout-button) – opens the Mobile Flyout panel. This option is only available with the [Mobile Flyout add-on](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/mobile-flyout/) installed.
3. A [Submit button](#add-a-submit-button) – redirects facet selections to a separate results page. This option is only available with the [Submit button add-on](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/submit-button/) installed.

Use the “Choose Type” setting to select the desired button type. Then change the button text and styling settings as needed.

Note:To translate the FacetWP Button widget’s button text when using WPML, [see the explanation below](#translate-facet-widget-headings-and-button-widget-labels-in-wpml).

#### Add an “Apply” button

When you add an “**Apply**” button to your page, it will disable FacetWP’s auto-refresh function and apply facet selections only when the button is clicked.

Adding this button type does exactly the same as explained in our tutorial on [how to disable facet auto-refresh and add an apply/submit button](/how-to-disable-facet-auto-refresh-and-add-an-apply-button/). It will also automatically add [the mentioned JavaScript snippet that disables auto-refresh](/how-to-disable-facet-auto-refresh-and-add-an-apply-button/#disable-auto-refresh) to your page.  As described in this tutorial, this button will disable auto-refresh for all [facet types](/help-center/facets/facet-types/), except for [Pager facets](/help-center/facets/facet-types/pager/) and [Reset facets](/help-center/facets/facet-types/reset/). If needed, you can re-enable auto-refresh for individual facets or facet types with 
```
FWP.refresh()
```

, for example [when using a Sort facet](/how-to-disable-facet-auto-refresh-and-add-an-apply-button/#re-enable-auto-refresh-for-sort-facets).

#### Add a “Flyout” button

To be able to create a Flyout button, first install the [Mobile Flyout add-on](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/mobile-flyout/). When you then add a “**Flyout**” button to your page, the button will open the Flyout panel, [as described here](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/mobile-flyout/#usage).

#### Add a “Submit” button

![The FacetWP Elementor Button widget Listing Page redirect setting.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/facetwp-elementor-add-on-button-widget-redirect-setting.png)The FacetWP Elementor Button widget Listing Page redirect setting.
To be able to create a Submit button, first install the [Submit button add-on](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/submit-button/).

The Submit button widget is a convenient alternative to adding the Submit button manually or with a shortcode, [as described in the Submit button add-on’s setup instructions](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/submit-button/#how-to-set-it-up).

After following the setup instructions and adding the Submit button widget, clicking the button will redirect the page to the URL specified in the “Listing Page” setting field of the widget. When facets on the page have selections, they will be included in the redirect.

Note:To translate the FacetWP Button widget’s button text and the Submit button’s listing page URL when using WPML, [see the explanation below](#translate-facet-widget-headings-and-button-widget-labels-in-wpml).

## Add FacetWP shortcodes with a Shortcode widget

![How to use an Elementor Shortcode widget to add facets or listings.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/03/elementor-shortcode-widget-add-facetwp-shortcode.png)How to use an Elementor Shortcode widget to add facets or listings.
Instead of [using FacetWP wigets](#facetwp-elementor-widgets), shortcodes for [facets](/help-center/facets/), [listing templates](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/) and [other features](/help-center/developers/shortcodes-reference/) can also be added with an Elementor Shortcode widget.

Just copy the shortcode from the FacetWP overview page or facet/listing settings page, and paste it into the widget.

As an alternative to a Shortcode widget, you can also use an HTML widget.

## Using FacetWP on WP archive templates

![Elementor archive widgets.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/03/archive_widgets.png)Elementor archive widgets.
FacetWP can also be enabled on WP archive templates created with the Elementor Theme builder.

The [Archive Posts widget](https://docs.elementor.com/article/464-archive-posts-widget-pro) (for posts, pages, and custom post types) and the [Archive Products widget](https://elementor.com/help/woocommerce-archive-products-pro/) (for [Woocommerce](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/woocommerce/) product archives) can be [enabled in their settings for FacetWP](#usage) in the same way as the other supported widgets, while automatically using the archive query for the results.

Note that in archive templates, facets may work even without enabling the “Enable FacetWP” setting on the widget, because of FacetWP’s query autodetection. However, to prevent issues with pagination, make sure to always [enable the “Enable FacetWP” setting](#usage), also in archive templates.

Important:If your post listing is a WP archive template, use an Archive Posts widget or an Archive Products widget, and **not** a “normal” Posts widget. Otherwise, FacetWP will detect and use the native archive query for filtering, causing a mismatch between your facets and the posts displayed in the listing. If you *do* use a normal Posts, Loop Grid, Woo Products, or Blog widget on your WP archive template, it is important to [set the query to use the native archive query](#using-a-posts-loop-grid-woo-products-or-blog-widget-on-a-wp-archive-template). If you intend to customize the query arguments (e.g. 
```
posts_per_page
```

 or using 
```
meta_query
```

) of an Archive Posts or Archive Products query, or a [widget set to use the “Current Query” (or “Main Query”)](#using-a-posts-loop-grid-woo-products-or-blog-widget-on-a-wp-archive-template), check the tutorial on [how to customize WordPress archive queries](/how-to-customize-wp-archive-queries/).

### Using a Posts, Loop Grid, Woo Products, or Blog widget on a WP archive template

![To use an Elementor Posts or Loop Grid widget, an UAE Posts or Woo-Products widget, or a PA Blog or Woo-Products widget on a WP archive page, set the 'Query' setting to use the 'Current Query' or 'Main Query'.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/10/elementor-current-query.png)To use an Elementor Posts or Loop Grid widget, an UAE Posts or Woo-Products widget, or a PA Blog or Woo-Products widget **on a WP archive page**, set the “Query” setting to use the “Current Query” or “Main Query”.
If you are using one of the following widgets **on a WP archive page** page, make sure to set it to use the native archive query that is already running on the page:

- [Elementor Posts widget](https://elementor.com/help/posts-widget-pro/)
- [Elementor Loop Grid widget](https://elementor.com/help/loop-grid/)
- [Ultimate Add-ons for Elementor Posts widget](https://ultimateelementor.com/widgets/posts/)
- [Ultimate Add-ons for Elementor Woo-Products widget](https://ultimateelementor.com/widgets/woocommerce-products/)
- [Premium Addons for Elementor Blog widget](https://premiumaddons.com/blog-widget-for-elementor-page-builder/)
- [Premium Addons for Elementor Woo-Products widget](https://premiumaddons.com/elementor-woocommerce-products/)

Depending on the widget, the native WP archive query can be set in Query > Source (or Query Type) > **Current Query** (or **Main Query**).

Important: Using “Current Query” (or “Main Query”) as Source in archive templates with FacetWP enabled is **required**. Don’t use any other query source in archive templates, as this will make FacetWP detect and use the wrong query for filtering, causing a mismatch between your facets and the posts displayed in the listing. If you intend to customize the query arguments (e.g. 
```
posts_per_page
```

 or using 
```
meta_query
```

) of a Posts, Loop Grid, Woo Products, or Blog widget with its query set to use the “Current Query” or “Main Query” (which is the native WP archive query), you need to use the 
```
pre_get_posts
```

 hook, and **not** [Elementor’s Custom Query Filter hook](#customize-the-elementor-posts-or-loop-grid-widget-query). Check the tutorial on [how to customize WordPress archive queries](/how-to-customize-wp-archive-queries/) for examples.

### Using a JetEngine Listing Grid Widget on a WP archive template

![Using JetEngine Listing Grid Widget on a WP archive page.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/07/jetengine-listing-grid-use-as-archive-template.png)Using JetEngine Listing Grid Widget on a WP archive page.
If you are using a [JetEngine Listing Grid Widget](https://crocoblock.com/widgets/listing-grid/) on a WP archive page, make sure to enable the setting “Use as Archive Template”.

This setting works similarly to the [“Source > Current Query” setting](#using-a-posts-loop-grid-woo-products-or-blog-widget-on-a-wp-archive-template) in native Elementor widgets. If enabled, the widget’s query settings will be ignored, and the native WP archive query that is already running on the page will be used instead.

### Using a FacetWP Listing widget on a WP archive template

This is not recommended. See [the explanation above](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/elementor/#use-a-facetwp-listing-widget-on-a-wp-archive-template).

## Using Elementor pagination

![Elementor Posts or Archive Posts widget pagination settings.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/12/elementor-posts-widget-pagination-settings.jpg)Elementor Posts or Archive Posts widget pagination settings.
If you are using Elementor’s pagination (which you can set in Posts, Archive Posts or Loop Grid widgets), FacetWP will detect this pagination and convert it for use with AJAX filtering.

However, this will only work for the pagination options that have **numbered pagination**.

FacetWP does **not** work with the “Load on Click” or “Infinite Scroll” pagination types. Choosing “Infinite Scroll” will also lead to JS errors in the Console. (Side note: [FacetWP will not work with any type of “Infinite load” pagination](/help-center/faq/does-facetwp-support-infinite-scroll/)).

![In Loop Grid pagination settings, use 'Page Reload' in the 'Load Type' setting. The 'AJAX' option will not work with FacetWP.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/08/elementor-loop-grid-ajax-pagination.png)If you are using a Loop Grid with pagination, choose “Page Reload” in its Pagination > Load Type setting. The “AJAX” option will not work with FacetWP.
Additionally, Loop Grid widgets have a “Load Type” setting in their Pagination setting. Make sure to choose “Page Reload”. The “AJAX” option will **not** work with FacetWP. FacetWP will detect Elementor pagination clicks and will use it to trigger AJAX loading itself.

If the integration with Elementor pagination is not working correctly, you can also use a [Pager facet](/help-center/facets/facet-types/pager/), which offers several pager types, like numbered pagination and a “Load more” type.

## Block FacetWP from using the wrong post widget query

![Add a block_facet Query ID in Elementor](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/03/elementor-query-id-block-facet.png)Add a 
```
block_facet
```

 Query ID in Elementor
If FacetWP is identifying the wrong query in Elementor, you can use the [query_id setting](https://developers.elementor.com/custom-query-filter/) to block FacetWP from using a post widget query that it shouldn’t.

Give the ‘Query ID’ setting in the Elementor widget a value of 
```
block_facet
```

, and add this code to your (child) theme’s functions.php:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More infoadd_action( 'elementor/query/block_facet', function( $query ) {
  if ( 0 !== strpos( $query->get( 'post_type'), 'acf' ) ) { // Prevent issues with this hook influencing ACF field based facets.
    $query->set( 'facetwp', false );
  }
} );
```

Note:The extra check in [line 2](#block-facet-L2) is to prevent [issues with this hook influencing the order of ACF field based facets](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/advanced-custom-fields/#fix-facet-choice-order-issues-when-using-an-acf-checkbox-field-and-elementor). You can remove this check if you don’t use Advanced Custom Fields.

## Force FacetWP to use a specific post widget query

![Add a use_facet Query ID in Elementor](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/03/elementor-query-id-use-facet.png)Add a 
```
use_facet
```

 Query ID in Elementor
You can force FacetWP to use a specific Elementor query ID. Give the ‘Query ID’ setting in the Elementor widget a value of 
```
use_facet
```

, and add this code to your (child) theme’s functions.php:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More infoadd_action( 'elementor/query/use_facet', function( $query ) {
  if ( 0 !== strpos( $query->get( 'post_type'), 'acf' ) ) { // Prevent issues with this hook influencing ACF field based facets.
    $query->set( 'facetwp', true );
  }
});

add_filter( 'facetwp_is_main_query', function( $is_main_query, $query ) {
  if ( true !== $query->get( 'facetwp', false ) ) {
    return false;
  }
  return $is_main_query;
}, 10, 2 );
```

Note:The extra check in [line 2](#use-facet-L2) is to prevent [issues with this hook influencing the order of ACF field based facets](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/advanced-custom-fields/#fix-facet-choice-order-issues-when-using-an-acf-checkbox-field-and-elementor). You can remove this check if you don’t use Advanced Custom Fields.

## Prevent page scroll with the AEP “Post Blocks Adv” widget

![Prevent scrolling on page load with the AnyWhere Elementor Pro Post Blocks Adv widget.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/aep-post-blocks-adv-disable-scroll-on-load.png)Prevent scrolling on page load with the AnyWhere Elementor Pro Post Blocks Adv widget.
If you are using the [AnyWhere Elementor Pro](https://www.elementoraddons.com/anywhere-elementor-pro/) “Post Blocks Adv” widget (which has [FacetWP integration](https://www.elementoraddons.com/aepro/anywhere-elementor-pro-facetwp-integration)), you may see the page scroll to the top of the results after interacting with facets.

This scroll on (AJAX) page load can be disabled by the “Disable Scroll to Top on Load” option, located under the “Pagination” section of the Post Blocks Adv widget.

Be aware that this Pagination section **can be hidden** if you happen to have the “Infinite Scroll” option enabled in the “Layout” section. If that is the case, disable the Infinite Scroll option, then go to the Pagination section and enable the “Disable Scroll to Top on Load” option.

It’s best to then **keep the Infinite Scroll option disabled** because FacetWP does not support any form of infinite scroll. The closest thing is the [Pager facet](/help-center/facets/facet-types/pager/), which has a pager type that generates a “load more” button, working similar to infinite scroll.

## Customize the Elementor Posts or Loop Grid widget query

If you are using [Elementor Pro](https://elementor.com/pro) with a [Posts widget](https://elementor.com/help/posts-widget-pro/) or a [Loop Grid widget](https://elementor.com/help/loop-grid/), and you need to adjust the query, you can use Elementor’s [Custom Query Filter hook](https://developers.elementor.com/docs/hooks/custom-query-filter/).

Important:If you are using a Posts widgets or Loop Grid widgets **on a WP archive template**, [with the Query > Source setting set to “Current Query”](#using-a-posts-loop-grid-woo-products-or-blog-widget-on-a-wp-archive-template), **don’t** use this hook, as it will not work correctly. Use WP’s 
```
pre_get_posts
```

 hook instead. Check the tutorial on [how to customize WordPress archive queries](/how-to-customize-wp-archive-queries/) for examples.
Elementor’s Custom Query Filter hook works similarly to WP’s 
```
pre_get_posts
```

 hook. It exposes the [WP_Query](https://developer.wordpress.org/reference/classes/wp_query/) object and allows you to customize the query in any way you want.

![How to give your widget's query a unique Query ID.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/07/elementor_query_id.png)How to give your widget’s query a unique Query ID.
In the following example, we add a fallback sorting method by overriding the original 
```
orderby
```

 query argument, and we change the default 
```
posts_per_page
```

 to 12. We also add an extra (optional) condition to let the hook run on category archive pages only.

Make sure to replace 
```
my_query_id
```

 with the **unique** Query ID you have set in the “Query ID” setting of the widget:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More infoadd_action('elementor/query/my_query_id', function($query) { // Replace "my_query_id" with your unique Query ID
  if ( 0 !== strpos( $query->get( 'post_type'), 'acf' ) ) { // Prevent issues with this hook influencing ACF field based facets.
    if ( $query->is_category() && $query->is_main_query() ) {  // Optional conditions
      $query->set( 'posts_per_page', 12 );
      $query->set( 'orderby', [
        'date'  => 'DESC', // Primary sort: by post date, descending (from new to old)
        'title' => 'ASC'   // Secondary, fallback sort: by post title, alphabetically (from A-Z)
      ] );
    }
  }
});
```

Note:This specific example of adding a fallback sorting method can be useful if you are trying to [fix duplicate results in your listing](/how-to-prevent-duplicate-results/).
Note:The extra check in [line 2](#change-query-elementor-L2) is to prevent [issues with this hook influencing the order of ACF field based facets](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/advanced-custom-fields/#fix-facet-choice-order-issues-when-using-an-acf-checkbox-field-and-elementor). You can remove this check if you don’t use Advanced Custom Fields.

### Add a custom “No results” message for Posts or Loop Grid widgets

To add a custom “No results” message when a Posts or Loop Grid widget does not return any results, add the following snippet to your (child) theme’s functions.php:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More infoadd_action('elementor/query/query_results', function($query) {
  $total = $query->found_posts;
  if ($total == 0) {
    echo '<h2>No results found.</h2>';
  }
});
```

For more elaborate ways to do this, check out [the answers in this forum thread](https://github.com/elementor/elementor/issues/7624), or consider using [this plugin](https://github.com/johnkraczek/post-not-found-for-elementor).

## Fix issues caused by Elementor’s Motion Effects options

There are several known incompatibilities with the following Advanced > Motion Effects pane options of Elementor sections/widgets:

### Do not use Elementor Pro’s “Sticky” option

![Do not use Elementor Pro's Sticky or Entrance Animation options.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/03/elementor-motion-effects.png)Do **not** use Elementor Pro’s “Sticky” or “Entrance Animation” options.
If you have [Elementor Pro](https://elementor.com/pro) installed, Elementor sections/widgets will have a “Sticky” option in their Advanced > Motion Effects options pane. The option creates a “[sticky scrolling](https://elementor.com/help/sticky-scrolling-effect-pro/)” effect.

**This “Sticky” option is incompatible with FacetWP** when used on sections/widgets containing facets or listings.

If this option is enabled, Elementor’s JavaScript will dynamically duplicate the section/widget in the code (invisibly) when the page is scrolled. This causes the facets or listings within the section/widget to be duplicated too, resulting in several issues with FacetWP.

One noticeable effect will be duplicate facets in the [Mobile Flyout](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/mobile-flyout/), if you are using that.

If you need a “sticky” effect on certain page elements, use a script that does not duplicate elements. Or better yet, create the effect by using the [position:sticky property](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/position) in CSS:

```
How to use custom CSS?CSS code can be placed in your (child) theme's style.css file. Alternatively, you can add it manually between <style> tags in the <head> section, in your (child) theme's header.php file. You can also load it with a hook in your (child) theme's functions.php file, or in the Custom Hooks add-on. To load the code only on pages with facets, use the facetwp_scripts hook. To load it on all pages, use wp_head or wp_footer.  Or you can use a code snippets plugin. More info.myelement {
  position: sticky;
  top: 20px;
}
```

This solution is also used on Help Center pages for the sticky right sidebar (which you can see on pages with a sidebar height less than the browser window height).

### Do not use Elementor’s “Entrance Animation” option

All Elementor sections/widgets have an “Entrance Animation” option in their Advanced > Motion Effects options pane. When enabled, this option sets the section/widget to be invisible and [lets it appear with an entrance animation](https://elementor.com/help/entrance-animations/) when you scroll down the page.

**Entrance Animations are incompatible with FacetWP** when used on widgets containing facets or listings. Enabling this option will lead to issues like posts not showing up after filtering.

## Do not use facets in Elementor popups

We regularly see users trying to get facets working within Elementor popups. Unfortunately, **using facets in Elementor popups is not supported**.

The closest alternative is the [Mobile Flyout add-on](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/mobile-flyout/) which was built specifically for mobile support.

Users have also reported success with adding facets to the [Off-Canvas widget by Ultimate Addons for Elementor](https://ultimateelementor.com/widgets/off-canvas/). Note that this widget works differently from FacetWP’s Mobile Flyout. The Mobile Flyout dynamically moves facets to the open flyout, so that all facets on the page (or [a selection](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/mobile-flyout/#customize-the-facets-and-facet-order-in-the-flyout)) are also available in the flyout. The Off-Canvas widget is always in the source code and moves off-canvas with CSS. So facets on the page are not necessarily in the off-canvas widget: you’ll have to decide which ones are in the widget and which ones are not. (And don’t add the same facet(s) both on the page as in the widget, as that will generally not work and cause filtering issues.)

## Fix issues caused by Elementor’s “Element Caching” feature

In version 3.22.0, Elementor [introduced](https://elementor.com/blog/elementor-322-Off-Canvas-Element-Caching/) a new experimental [Element Caching setting](https://elementor.com/help/element-caching-help/). In version 3.32.0, “Element Cache” was promoted to a normal setting and moved to the Performance tab.

Be warned that **Element Caching is incompatible with FacetWP**. Depending on the type of widget this setting is enabled on, Element Caching can cause the following issues:

- No facets appearing at all.
- Disappearing facets after one use.
- A [“FacetWP was unable to auto-detect the post listing” error](/help-center/faq/why-am-i-seeing-facetwp-was-unable-to-auto-detect-the-post-listing/) when using facets.

To prevent or fix these issues, make sure to **disable Element Cache** globally in Elementor > Settings > Performance > Element Cache:

![How to disable Elementor Element Cache globally.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/elementor-element-cache.png)How to disable Elementor Element Cache globally.
![Disable Elementor Element Cache per widget in the Advanced tab >  Layout.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/elementor-widget-cache-settings.png)Disable Elementor Element Cache per widget in the Advanced tab > Layout.
Alternatively, if you want to keep this global setting enabled, make sure that all FacetWP-related Elementor widgets have their individual **“Cache Settings” setting set to “Inactive”**. Or you can leave it at “Default” if the default state is “Inactive”. This setting can be found on any widget’s Advanced tab, under Layout. The default state is mentioned underneath it and should show “Inactive” for all [supported widgets](#supported-widgets). We’ve seen it being “Active” for some widgets in older installs, so watch out for this and set it explicitly to “Inactive” if needed.

**Element Caching needs to be “Inactive” for every FacetWP-related widget**: Shortcode widgets, Text Editor widgets, and HTML widgets containing FacetWP facet or listing shortcodes, FacetWP’s own [Facet widget](#the-facetwp-facet-widget) and [Listing widget](#the-facetwp-listing-widget), and any [FacetWP-enabled posts- and loop grid widget](#supported-widgets).

### The “Element Cache is active” warning

![The 'Element Cache is active' warning.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/elementor-element-cache-warning.png)The “Element Cache is active” warning.
To prevent the [above-mentioned issues](#fix-issues-caused-by-elementors-element-caching-feature), in version 1.9.4+ of the add-on, a red “Element Cache is active” warning was added in the “FacetWP” section of supported Elementor widgets. The warning appears if the Element Cache is globally enabled and the current widget’s “Cache Settings” setting is explicitly set to “Active” in Advanced > Layout.

If you see this warning, [disable Element Cache globally in the settings](#fix-issues-caused-by-elementors-element-caching-feature), or for the widget itself by setting it to “Inactive” in the “Cache Settings” setting.

In some cases, FacetWP cannot generate the necessary warning:

- when the default state of the widget is “Active”. We’ve only seen this in older installs.
- 
- in Shortcode, HTML, Text Editor and similar widgets, which could contain FacetWP shortcodes.
- in [AnyWhere Elementor Pro](https://www.elementoraddons.com/anywhere-elementor-pro/) [Post Blocks](https://aedocs.wpvibes.com/article/8-post-blocks) and [Post Blocks Adv](https://aedocs.wpvibes.com/article/125-ae-post-blocks-adv) widgets, as their integration [is built into AEP](https://www.elementoraddons.com/aepro/anywhere-elementor-pro-facetwp-integration).

Keep an eye out for these cases, as these widgets still need their “Cache Settings” setting set to **“Inactive”** (or [disable Element Cache globally in the settings](#fix-issues-caused-by-elementors-element-caching-feature)).

## Fix the Elementor JSON error

Note: This issue was **fixed** by Elementor in version **3.20.0**, released on March 11, 2024. The below instructions are only for Elementor versions between 3.17 and 3.19.4.
If you are using Elementor 3.17 – 3.19.4, you will likely encounter an issue with facets not working. The cause is a JSON error occurring in the AJAX refresh data returned to FacetWP, which in turn causes FacetWP to be unable to process the response.

This error is caused by an incompatibility with a new feature of Elementor called “Optimize Image Loading”, which seems to be enabled by default. We are [trying to get Elementor to solve the cause of the issue](https://github.com/elementor/elementor/issues/24226).

Currently, there are three ways to fix this issue:

1. You can **disable** the “Optimize Image Loading” setting in the Elementor settings here:

Elementor > Settings > Features > Optimize Image Loading.

![Disable Elementor's 'Optimize Image Loading' setting to prevent a JSON error.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/02/elementor-disable-optimize-image-loading.png)Disable Elementor’s ‘Optimize Image Loading’ setting to prevent a JSON error.
1. Or, you can install or update our Elementor integration addon. Version 1.9+ contains a fix.
2. Or, you can add the following snippet to your (child) theme’s functions.php:​

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More infoadd_action( 'shutdown', function() {
  if ( isset( FWP()->request ) && FWP()->request->is_refresh ) {
    $buffer = ob_get_status();
    if ( 'Elementor\Modules\ImageLoadingOptimization\Module::handle_buffer_content' == $buffer['name'] ) {
      ob_end_flush();
    }
  }
}, - 1 );
```

## Using an Elementor Loop Item template in a Listing Builder listing

It is possible to use an Elementor (Pro) Loop Item template directly in the loop of a FacetWP Listing Builder listing, by [using the listing in Dev mode](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/using-the-listing-builder-in-dev-mode/).

First, you need to create a Loop Item. You can do this by [setting up a Loop Grid](https://elementor.com/help/how-do-i-build-a-loop-grid/) on any page. In that process, you’ll have to create a Loop Item. Delete the Loop Grid widget from the page after that. If you keep it for some reason, do **not** [enable FacetWP on it](#usage), as you’re going to use a FacetWP Listing Builder listing instead.

You can also create a Loop Item template directly, by going to Templates, then click “Add new template” and selecting “Loop Item” as template type.

Existing Loop Item templates can be found by going to the Templates overview page, then clicking the Loop Item tab. Or use this URL directly:

```
https://yourdomain.com/wp-admin/edit.php?post_type=elementor_library&tabs_group&elementor_library_type=loop-item
```

With the Loop Item template ready, [create a FacetWP Listing Builder listing](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/), [set up the Query tab](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/#using-the-query-tab) however you like, and [set the Display tab to Dev mode](https://facetwp.com/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/using-the-listing-builder-in-dev-mode/#how-to-use-display-code-in-dev-mode). In the Display Code field, paste the following template:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More info<?php
    $my_loop_item_template_id = 8362; // Specifify the Elementor Loop Item template ID to use
?>

<?php if ( have_posts() ) : ?>
    <?php while ( have_posts() ): the_post();

       echo render_elementor_loop_item_in_custom_loop( $my_loop_item_template_id, get_the_ID() ); // Display the Elementor Loop Item for this post

    endwhile; ?>
<?php else : ?>
    <p><?php _e( 'Sorry, no posts matched your criteria.' ); ?></p>
<?php endif; ?>
```

Change the ID in [line 2](#loop-item-template-L2) to your Loop Item’s template ID. You can find this ID in the shortcode in the right column on the above-mentioned Templates overview page. Or hover over the item, and check your browser’s status bar, which will show the ID as e.g. 
```
post=8362
```

.

Next, paste the following function into your functions.php, or in a snippet in a code snippets plugin:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More infofunction render_elementor_loop_item_in_custom_loop( $template_id, $post_id ) {

    // Ensure Elementor is loaded
    if ( ! did_action( 'elementor/loaded' ) ) {
        return '<!-- Elementor is not loaded. -->';
    }

    // Get the Elementor frontend instance
    $elementor_frontend = \Elementor\Plugin::instance()->frontend;

    // Store the original global $post object and setup postdata
    global $post;
    $original_post = $post; // Store original post object to restore later

    // Set the global $post object to the specific post we want to render
    // This is crucial for Elementor's Dynamic Tags (e.g., Post Title, Post Excerpt)
    // to pull data from the correct post in the loop.
    $current_post_for_rendering = get_post( $post_id );
    if ( ! $current_post_for_rendering ) {
        return '<!-- Post ID ' . $post_id . ' not found for Elementor rendering. -->';
    }

    $post = $current_post_for_rendering;
    setup_postdata( $post ); // Required for WordPress template tags to work correctly

    // Render the Elementor template content
    // 'get_builder_content_for_display' is the recommended method for frontend rendering.
    $with_css = true; // Retrieve Loop Item content with or without CSS?
    $content = $elementor_frontend->get_builder_content_for_display( $template_id, $with_css );

    // Restore the original global $post object and reset postdata
    wp_reset_postdata(); // Resets WordPress's internal post data
    $post = $original_post; // Restore the original post object

    return $content;
}
```

In [line 28](#retrieve-loop-item-L28), you can determine if the Loop Item content should be retrieved with or without its own CSS, by setting this to 
```
true
```

 or 
```
false
```

.

Last but not least, add your FacetWP listing to the page with a [Listing widget](#the-facetwp-listing-widget), or by pasting its shortcode into a Shortcode wiget. The page should now show your posts in the layout of the Loop Item. Add any extra styling, like (responsive) columns or grid, or spacing with custom CSS.

## Translate Facet widget headings and Button widget labels in WPML

![The WPML Translation Editor page setting.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/wpml_translation_editor_page_setting.png)The “WPML Translation Editor” page setting.
If you are using FacetWP with the Elementor add-on (v1.9.4+ required), WPML, and [the Multilingual add-on](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/multilingual/), and you are translating your page(s) with the [WPML Advanced Translation Editor](https://wpml.org/documentation/translating-your-contents/advanced-translation-editor/) (chosen with the middle option in the “Translation this Document” page setting — as shown in the image on the right), the following items will show up in the Translation Editor to be translated:

- Heading texts set in any [FacetWP Facet widget](#the-facetwp-facet-widget) on the page.
- Button text labels set in any [FacetWP Button widget](#the-facetwp-button-widget) on the page.
- Listing page URLs set in any FacetWP Button widget on the page that is [set to the “Submit button” button type](#add-a-submit-button).

These translatable items will look like this:

![Translatable text/labels of FacetWP Elementor widgets in the WPML Advanced Translation Editor.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/wpml_advanced_translation_editor_elementor_modules.png)Translatable text/labels of FacetWP Elementor widgets in the WPML Advanced Translation Editor.
The alternative is to translate these items in the Elementor editor of the translated page itself (with the “Translate this Document” page setting set to “WordPress Editor” (the option on the right)).

Note:The [FacetWP Facet widget’s Header text](#the-facetwp-facet-widget) is also translatable with [the facetwp_18n hook](/help-center/developers/hooks/advanced-hooks/facetwp_i18n/).
Note:If you are using the [Mobile Flyout add-on](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/mobile-flyout/), the facet headings/labels that [are automatically created](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/mobile-flyout/#facet-headings-labels) can be translated with the [facetwp_i18n hook](/help-center/developers/hooks/advanced-hooks/facetwp_i18n/).

### How to translate FacetWP widgets in Elementor templates with WPML

To get the [above-described](#translate-facet-widget-headings-and-button-widget-labels-in-wpml) way of translating FacetWP Facet and Button widgets working with an Elementor **template**, Elementor’s “My templates” (
```
elementor_library
```

) post type must be set to “Translatable” in WPML > Settings > Post Types Translation:

![How to make Elementor templates translatable with WPML](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/elementor_translate_template.png)How to make Elementor templates translatable with WPML
![The WPML Translation Editor page setting.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/wpml_translation_editor_page_setting.png)The “WPML Translation Editor” page setting.
Then, in the Elementor template’s WP edit screen, the “Translate this Document” setting should be set to the middle option: “WPML Translation Editor” (as shown in the image on the right), just like for normal pages.

Next, you can add/edit the translation for the template by clicking the + (or cog) icon next to the language(s) below it. Or, go to Elementor > Editor > Templates > Saved Templates, click “All”, find your template, and click the cog icon next to its title. Both will take you to WPML’s Advanced Translation Editor, which will look like [the screenshot above](#wpml-advanced-translation-editor).

As mentioned before, the [FacetWP Facet widget’s Header text](#the-facetwp-facet-widget) is also translatable with [the facetwp_18n hook](/help-center/developers/hooks/advanced-hooks/facetwp_i18n/)

## Fast Velocity Minify

**Source:** [https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/fast-velocity-minify/](https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/fast-velocity-minify/)

The [Fast Velocity Minify plugin](https://wordpress.org/plugins/fast-velocity-minify/ target=) has a setting under “HTML Options” to strip HTML comments:

![Fast Velocity Minify - strip HTML comments](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/fastvelocityminify-hmtlminification.png)
This setting also strips HTML comments from listing pages. On all [types of listing templates](/help-center/listing-templates/) (except ones that use a FacetWP [shortcode template](/help-center/listing-templates/listing-builder/)) this will cause problems, because FacetWP needs the 
```
<!--fwp-loop-->
```

 HTML comment to automatically add a 
```
facetwp-template
```

 class on the element surrounding the results. This class is needed to dynamically replace the HTML within this element when filtering. Without this class, FacetWP does not know which content to dynamically replace.

Turning off HTML minification fixes this problem. However, if you want to keep HTML minification, you also can also add the 
```
facetwp-template
```

 class to your templates manually. Simply [add the class to the parent element of your post loop](/help-center/listing-templates/wp-archive-page/#manually-add-the-facetwp-template-class). On WooCommerce shop pages, you can use [these hooks](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/woocommerce/#fix-woocommerce-pagination-and-result-counts) that do the same.

## Flatsome (theme)

**Source:** [https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/flatsome/](https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/flatsome/)

[Flatsome](https://themeforest.net/item/flatsome-multipurpose-responsive-woocommerce-theme/5484319) is a very popular (and well-built) WooCommerce theme, and with a few steps you can easily integrate it with FacetWP.

## Create the facets

First, create the facets to use on the Flatsome shop page. Go into 
```
Settings > FacetWP > Facets
```

, and click the “Add New” button.

For each facet’s 
```
Data source
```

, select a choice underneath the “WooCommerce” dropdown header.

![](https://i.imgur.com/orc36XT.gif)

## Install the integration add-on

When you install our free Flatsome integration add-on (link in the sidebar) it automatically prepares FacetWP for your Shop page.

The add-on fixes a few compatibility issues with the Flatsome theme, including (a) result counts not updating and (b) image lightbox and other interactivity not working after ajax refreshes.

All that’s left is to add your facet shortcodes into some sidebar text widgets (see below).

## Add facets to the Shop Sidebar widget

Browse to 
```
Appearance > Widgets
```

, then add a new Text widget to the “Shop Sidebar” region. Give it a label, then enter a facet shortcode into the Content box:

![](https://i.imgur.com/0MpY8Cl.gif)

That’s it, your Flatsome site is now FacetWP-enabled 🙂

## Genesis Framework

**Source:** [https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/genesis-framework/](https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/genesis-framework/)

If you’re trying to integrate FacetWP with the [Genesis Framework](https://www.studiopress.com/themes/genesis/) [using the CSS class approach](https://facetwp.com/help-center/listing-templates/custom-wp-query/), you need to add the 
```
facetwp-template
```

 class to a container element surrounding your post loop.

The following snippet adds the class to the Genesis “content” element:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More infofunction custom_facetwp_class( $atts ) {
  $atts['class'] .= ' facetwp-template';
  return $atts;
}
add_filter( 'genesis_attr_content', 'custom_facetwp_class' );
```

## Google Analytics 4

**Source:** [https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/google-analytics-4/](https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/google-analytics-4/)

With [Google Analytics 4](https://developers.google.com/analytics/devguides/collection/ga4), it’s very easy to track how users are interacting with facets:

## Set up Google Analytics 4 for your website

The rest of this guide assumes that you have set up Google Analytics for your website. In short, the following steps are needed. If you are setting up a new property, you will be automatically guided through these steps. For existing properties, check the mentioned settings:

1. Create a Google Analytics 4 account and a Property for your website.
2. Create a “Web” Data Stream for your website. For an existing property you can find this under Admin > Property > Data Streams > Web.
3. Make sure to enable “Enhanced measurement” in the process. You can find this setting later by navigating to Admin > Property > Data Streams, and then selecting your website’s Data Stream.
4. If you are setting up a new property, the setup instructions will then guide you to the Google tag snippet. If you need to look up this tag snippet later, it can be found by navigating to Admin > Property > Data Streams > Web, and then selecting your websites Data Stream. In the next window, go to Google tag > Configure tag settings > Your Google tag > Installation instructions.
5. Copy the Google tag snippet and paste it at the bottom of your website’s 
```
<head>
```

 section, which can be found in your theme’s header.php file.

A default Google Analytics 4 
```
gtag()
```

 snippet looks as follows (replace 
```
XXXXXXXXXX
```

 with your “Measurement ID”):

```
<!-- Google tag (gtag.js) -->
<script async src="https://www.googletagmanager.com/gtag/js?id=G-XXXXXXXXXX"></script>
<script>
  window.dataLayer = window.dataLayer || [];
  function gtag(){dataLayer.push(arguments);}
  gtag('js', new Date());

  gtag('config', 'G-XXXXXXXXXX');
</script>
```

## Measuring pageviews

There are basically two ways to [measure pageviews](https://developers.google.com/analytics/devguides/collection/ga4/views?client_type=gtag) with facet selections. The first is the easiest. If you have followed the [above setup instructions](#set-up-google-analytics-4-for-your-website), you have to do nothing else:

### Method 1: Track facet pages and history states

![Google Analytics 4 Data Streams.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/06/ga4-data-streams.png)
First, make sure “Enhanced measurement” is enabled, if you did not do so already while [setting up your property](#set-up-google-analytics-4-for-your-website). You can find this setting later by navigating to Admin > Property > Data Streams, and then selecting your website’s Data Stream:

![Enabling Google Analytics 4 'Advanced measurement' setting.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/06/ga4-advanced-measurement.png)Enabling Google Analytics 4 ‘Advanced measurement’ setting.
Next, click on the cog icon in the bottom right of this setting, and under “Page views”, click “Show advanced settings”. Make sure that “Page changes based on browser history events” is **enabled**, which it is by default:

![Enabling Google Analytics 4 'Advanced measurement' - 'Page changes based on browser history events' setting.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/06/ga4-advanced-measurement-history-states.png)Enabling Google Analytics 4 ‘Advanced measurement > Page changes based on browser history events’ setting.
**That’s it**. With this setting enabled, a pageview is tracked when the browser [history state](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/History_API) changes, which happens when a user clicks the browser’s back or forward button. When the page URL changes after filtering with facets, the history state *also* changes, so these pageviews will be tracked too.

### Method 2: Track facet pages but not history states

If you want to track facet pages, but **don’t** want to track pageviews triggered by changes in the history state (using the browser’s back or forward buttons), you can use the following method. This method also works if you want to keep the “Enhanced measurement” setting entirely disabled.

![Google Analytics 4 Data Streams.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/06/ga4-data-streams.png)
First, navigate to Admin > Property > Data Streams, and then select your website’s Data Stream. Click on the cog icon in the bottom right of this setting, and under “Page views”, click “Show advanced settings”. Make sure that “Page changes based on browser history events” is **disabled**:

![Disabling Google Analytics 4 'Advanced measurement' - 'Page changes based on browser history events' setting.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/06/ga4-advanced-measurement-history-states-disabled.png)Disabling Google Analytics 4 ‘Advanced measurement > Page changes based on browser history events’ setting.
Without this setting, history states will not be tracked anymore. This includes dynamic pageviews with facet selections. To track these, you can “manually” trigger a pageview by adding the following snippet to your site’s 
```
<head>
```

 section (which can be found in your theme’s header.php file), below your [tag snippet](#ga4-embed):

```
<script>
  document.addEventListener('facetwp-loaded', function() {
    if (FWP.loaded) {
      gtag('event', 'page_view');
    }
  });
</script>
```

This snippet uses the [facetwp_loaded event](/help-center/developers/javascript-reference/facetwp-loaded/), which fires after each facet interaction. Before sending the pageview, it checks for 
```
FWP.loaded
```

 which is only 
```
true
```

 *after* the initial page load (which will already be tracked by Google Analytics).

Warning: If you send manual pageviews with the above snippet, **without disabling** “Page changes based on browser history events” under “Enhanced measurement”, you will end up with **duplicate pageviews**.

### Disable tracking of facet pages

If you **don’t** want to track pageviews when selecting facets, navigate to Admin > Property > Data Streams, and then select your website’s Data Stream. Click on the cog icon in the bottom right of this setting, and under “Page views”, click “Show advanced settings”. Make sure that “Page changes based on browser history events” is **disabled**:

![Disabling Google Analytics 4 'Advanced measurement' - 'Page changes based on browser history events' setting.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/06/ga4-advanced-measurement-history-states-disabled.png)Disabling Google Analytics 4 ‘Advanced measurement > Page changes based on browser history events’ setting.
With this setting disabled, and no [manual triggering of pageviews](#ga4-trigger-pageview), both history states (using the browser’s back or forward buttons) and dynamic pageviews with facet selections will not be tracked anymore.

Note that a *direct* page (re)load of a URL with a facet selection in it **will be tracked** because it does not change the history state.

## Verify pageview measuring

![Google Analytics 4 DebugView.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/06/ga4-debug-view.png)
If you want to verify that facet pages and/or history states (using the browser’s back or forward buttons) are measured properly, the best way to do so is by using [Google Analytics’ “DebugView”](https://support.google.com/analytics/answer/7201382). The DebugView feature can be found in your website’s Property menu, at the bottom.

DebugView lets you see live pageviews coming in (with a slight delay of ~20 seconds). For each pageview, you can see its parameters. The most useful one is 
```
page_location
```

, which will show the full URL, including all URL parameters if the page has facet selections:

![Verifying live pageviews with Google Analytics 4 DebugView.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/06/ga4-debugview.png)Verifying live pageviews with Google Analytics 4 DebugView.
To be able to use DebugView, you need to enable Google Analytic’s Debug Mode. This can be done [in multiple ways](https://support.google.com/analytics/answer/7201382), but the easiest is by temporarily setting it as a parameter in your Google tag snippet, as follows:

```
<!-- Google tag (gtag.js) -->
<script async src="https://www.googletagmanager.com/gtag/js?id=G-XXXXXXXXXX"></script>
<script>
  window.dataLayer = window.dataLayer || [];
  function gtag(){dataLayer.push(arguments);}
  gtag('js', new Date());

  gtag('config', 'G-XXXXXXXXXX', { 'debug_mode':true });
</script>
```

If you are using the [second method above](#method-2-track-facet-pages-but-not-history-states), you’ll also need to add this to the second snippet that triggers dynamic pageviews:

```
<script>
  document.addEventListener('facetwp-loaded', function() {
    if (FWP.loaded) {
      gtag('event', 'page_view', { 'debug_mode':true });
    }
  });
</script>
```

If you’re done verifying, make sure to disable Debug Mode again by removing the 
```
debug_mode
```

 parameter from the snippet(s). Note that setting the parameter to 
```
false
```

 does not disable it.

    
## See also

    - [The facetwp-loaded event](https://facetwp.com/help-center/developers/javascript-reference/facetwp-loaded/)

## Image Optimization by Optimole

**Source:** [https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/image-optimization-by-optimole/](https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/image-optimization-by-optimole/)

If you are using the [Image Optimization by Optimole plugin](https://wordpress.org/plugins/optimole-wp/) together with FacetWP, make sure to connect the plugin with an Optimole API key:

![Using Image Optimization by Optimole without an API key will lead to 500 server errors.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/06/optimole-no-api-key.png)Using Image Optimization by Optimole without an API key will lead to 500 server errors.
If you don’t do this, and leave the plugin with the above intro screen, your facet pages will suffer from 
```
500
```

 server errors when facets are used.

To prevent these fatal errors, make sure to connect Optimole with an API key, or deactivate the plugin. With an API installed, the intro screen should look like this:

![Use Image Optimization by Optimole with an API key to prevent fatal errors.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/06/optimole-connected-with-api-key.png)Use Image Optimization by Optimole with an API key to prevent fatal errors.

                    Last updated: June 27, 2023

## Incompatible plugins and themes

**Source:** [https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/incompatible-plugins-and-themes/](https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/incompatible-plugins-and-themes/)

Below is a list of issues and incompatibilities with certain [plugins](#incompatible-plugins) and [themes](#incompatible-themes).

When an issue is listed here, it does not by definition mean the whole plugin/theme is incompatible, or that the issue cannot be fixed. We mention possible fixes where we can, and try to keep this list up to date. However, we cannot re-test every incompatibility over time. Also, some issues may have been fixed in the meantime by the plugin/theme authors. Make sure to test things yourself before making any decisions based on this list.

[Contact support](/help-center/get-support/) if you have any questions about specific issues listed below. To give you more context we can also look up support tickets in which the issue originally came up.

Please let us know if you encounter any new incompatibilities that could be added to the list for the benefit of other users.

Don’t forget to check our “[Using FacetWP with…](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/)” section for info on using FacetWP together with often-used plugins. These pages describe all common issues and their fixes, and are kept up-to-date as much as possible.

Note:Please disregard the [#] numbers, they are for internal referencing.

## Incompatible plugins and known plugin issues

- Advanced Post Types Order – several incompatibilities [#24412]. FacetWP [is compatible with the free Post Types Order](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/post-types-order/) plugin.
- [Advanced Woo Search](https://wordpress.org/plugins/advanced-woo-search/) – This plugin has its own integration with FacetWP that causes issues with specific products not showing up when using facets. Contact the author if you experience any issues [#29805]
- [Ajax Search Lite](https://wordpress.org/plugins/ajax-search-lite/) – conflicts with FacetWP on the search results page, leading to non-functional facets [#29473, #29806]
- Asset CleanUp Pro – turn off “Manage in the front-end”, or facets won’t work when logged in
- [Custom Fields for WooCommerce](https://woocommerce.com/products/custom-fields-for-woocommerce/) by Addify – facet templates being unable to load on page load [#26116]
- [Custom Permalinks for WooCommerce](https://meow-crew.com/plugin/custom-permalinks-for-woocommerce) by Meow Crew [#23304, #23356, #26740, #31341] – plugin causes pagination redirects that break FacetWP’s Pager facet and listing refresh. Plugin author notified 2022-03-04. [Disabling the “301 redirects” setting fixes the issue](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/woocommerce-plugins/custom-permalinks-for-woocommerce/).
- Easy Content Types – plugin appears abandoned, killing query with “0 = 1” SQL [#22833]
- [EditorsKit – Gutenberg Block Editor Toolkit](https://wordpress.org/plugins/block-options/) – breaks Query Loop blocks (posts not refreshing) with the FacetWP Blocks add-on. [Issue posted here](https://wordpress.org/support/topic/re-render-of-block-in-function-hide-title-php-breaks-facetwp/). [#29639].
- ElasticPress – [set “facetwp_use_search_relevancy” = false](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/elasticpress/) [#11482]
- Elementor – using animations in Advanced > Motion Effects > Entrance Animation. See [this explanation](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/elementor/#do-not-use-elementors-entrance-animation-option).
- Elementor – using popups. FacetWP does not support using facets within Elementor popups. The closest alternative is the [Mobile Flyout add-on](/help-center/add-on-features-and-extras/mobile-flyout/) which was built specifically for mobile support.
- Elementor Pro – adding facets or listings in a “sticky” widget, set in Advanced > Motion Effects > Sticky [#17667, #23945, #26393]. See [this explanation](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/elementor/#do-not-use-elementor-pros-sticky-option).
- Facebook Pixel – interferes with keyword search [#9456]
- [Image Optimization by Optimole](https://wordpress.org/plugins/optimole-wp/) – if used without an API key, facet pages will have 500 server errors on refresh. The issue be fixed by [connecting with an API key](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/image-optimization-by-optimole/).
- [Linguise](https://wordpress.org/plugins/linguise/) [#24664]
- Login Require Press
- NextGEN Gallery [#9201]
- Password Protected [#10158]
- Popup Builder
- [Product Filter for WooCommerce by WBW](https://wordpress.org/plugins/woo-product-filter/). This filtering plugin causes [“The index table is empty” errors](/help-center/indexing/common-indexing-issues/#fix-a-the-index-table-is-empty-error). [#32184]
- [Request a Quote for WooCommerce](https://woocommerce.com/products/request-a-quote-plugin-for-woocommerce/) by Addify. The exact issue unclear. Try enabling “Advanced query detection” in FacetWP’s settings. (This setting was called “Strict query detection” in FacetWP versions older than [v4.5](/help-center/changelog/#4-5)). [#23222, #29719]
- [Scalability Pro](https://www.superspeedyplugins.com/product/scalability-pro/) by Superspeedy plugins. Enabling the [“Remove SQL_CALC_FOUND_ROWS” setting](https://www.superspeedyplugins.com/kb/scalability-pro/getting-started/scalability-pro-installation-and-options/#remove-sql-calc-found-rows-calctotals) (visible as 
```
spro_remove_sql_calc_found_rows
```

 query argument in Debug Mode) breaks FacetWP’s Pager + Load more facets. [#30145]
- [Self-hosted Google Fonts](https://wordpress.org/plugins/selfhost-google-fonts/) (causes JSON errors, Oxygen compatibility issue). This plugin is abandoned. Use an alternative like [Local Google Fonts](https://wordpress.org/plugins/local-google-fonts/), or see [this tutorial](https://www.wpbeginner.com/wp-tutorials/how-to-host-local-fonts-in-wordpress-for-a-faster-website/). [#17150, #28261, #30787, #30419]
- Shortcode Pagination for WooCommerce (plugin has been abandoned)
- Simple Custom Post Order (it’s setting 0 = 1 for query) [#14448]
- Search Everything [#8782]
- [Tag Manager – Header, Body And Footer](https://wordpress.org/plugins/tag-manager-header-body-footer/) by YYDevelopment – causes JSON errors by unclosed ob_start buffer: developer notified and unresponsive here: [here](https://wordpress.org/support/topic/json-errors-by-unclosed-ob_start-function/) [#26657]
- Theme My Login – “FacetWP was unable to auto-detect the post listing” error when using a WP_Query listing, probably caused by the plugin using “action” as redirect word in 
```
add_rewrite_tag();
```

 [#30284]
- Toolset Access – facets not working logged-out or with certain roles. Unknown cause [#23808, #24168]
- [WeGlot](https://wordpress.org/plugins/weglot/). FacetWP does not have an official integration with Weglot, and there are no current plans to officially support it. WPML and Polyang are supported, with our [Multilingual add-on](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/multilingual/), so you could consider using one of those. That said, we’ve seen users using Weglot successfully with FacetWP, with [the fixes described on this page](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/weglot/).  [#14894, #26222, #31113]
- WooCommerce (only multisite installs) – shop orders not visible [#10879]
- WooCommerce TM Extra Product Options [#12048]
- WooCommerce Product Bundles [#22498, #27358] – Wrong query detected when there is a bundle in the cart. [Enabling “Advanced query detection” in FacetWP’s settings fixes the issue](https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/woocommerce-plugins/woocommerce-product-bundles/). (This setting was called “Strict query detection” in FacetWP versions older than [v4.5](/help-center/changelog/#4-5)).
- WooCommerce Product Search – pagination / number of posts not working on product category/term pages, with both WooCommerce pagination and Pager faces [#18177, #21765, #21319, #23785, #26220].  There is [a workaround](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/woocommerce-product-search/).
- WordFence – 403 issue – [https://wordpress.org/support/topic/i-get-a-403-error-when-inserting-a-tag-via-elementor/](https://wordpress.org/support/topic/i-get-a-403-error-when-inserting-a-tag-via-elementor/) [#15256, #15378]
- WordPress Social Login
- WP Cerber (disable REST API blocking)
- WP Extended Search modifies facet’s query incorrectly [#14678]
- WP External Links – Empty ajax response after clicking facets or pagination [#22415, #21378, FWP-187]. There is [a fix](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/wp-external-links/).
- WP FAQ Pro (breaks FacetWP’s update checks)
- wpForo – forums plugin – causes issues with main query detection [#16192, #16163]
- WPMU Smush Pro (lazy load) [#14815]
- WISDM Reports for LearnDash – filtering not working for logged-in users [#25512]
- YITH cart messages premium [#5189]

## Incompatible themes and known theme issues

- Divi – issues with the “Grab the first post image” Divi setting. If your module or listing is showing the wrong post image, try disabling this setting in Divi > Theme Options [#5704, #31435]
- [My Listing theme](https://themeforest.net/item/mylisting-directory-listing-wordpress-theme/20593226) by 27collective (available on Themeforest) – This theme loads its own Vue scripts on every admin page, which breaks FacetWP settings. E.g. the Hierarchy Select facet does not show its “Show depth levels” setting [#29771]
- Neve theme issues with Elementor – [https://wordpress.org/support/topic/on-search-results-in-a-blank-page-with-a-white-rectangle/](https://wordpress.org/support/topic/on-search-results-in-a-blank-page-with-a-white-rectangle/)
- Salient theme – can be fixed by plugging the 
```
change_wp_search_size
```

 function [#9985]

## Intuitive Custom Post Order

**Source:** [https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/intuitive-custom-post-order/](https://facetwp.com/help-center/using-facetwp-with/intuitive-custom-post-order/)

![Intuitive Custom Post Order logo](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/icpto-logo.png)
If you are using the [Intuitive Custom Post Order](https://wordpress.org/plugins/intuitive-custom-post-order/) plugin to order the taxonomy terms for one or more taxonomies, make sure to go through the setup instructions below to preserve that order for facets that use these taxonomies as their data source, and have “Term order” set in their “Sort by” setting.

## Setup

1. First, create (or edit) a [facet](/help-center/facets/) and set its [data source](/help-center/facets/facet-types/checkboxes/#data-source) to a [taxonomy](/help-center/developers/facetwp-and-taxonomies/). You’re going to order the terms of this taxonomy with Intuitive CPO.
2. ![The 'Sort by > Term order' facet setting.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/sort_by_term_order.png)The “Sort by > Term order” facet setting.
Set the facet’s [“Sort by” option to “Term order”](/help-center/facets/facet-types/checkboxes/#sort-by). Only the following facet types have this setting: [Checkboxes](/help-center/facets/facet-types/checkboxes/), [Dropdown](/help-center/facets/facet-types/dropdown/), [Radio](/help-center/facets/facet-types/radio/), [fSelect](/help-center/facets/facet-types/fselect/), [Hierarchy](/help-center/facets/facet-types/hierarchy/), [Hierarchy Select](/help-center/facets/facet-types/hierarchy-select/), [Color](/help-center/facets/facet-types/color/), and [Exclude](/help-center/facets/facet-types/exclude/).
3. Next, in Settings > Intuitive CPO > Sortable Taxonomies, enable all taxonomies that you want to order with this plugin.
4. Add one of the two snippets below to your (child) theme’s functions.php. Which one depends on if you are [using WooCommerce](/help-center/using-facetwp-with/woocommerce/), and if so, how you want to order product categories.

![Enabling 'Product categories' in the 'Sortable Taxonomies' setting.](https://facetwp.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/intuitive-cpo-sortable-taxonomies.png)Enabling “Product categories” in the “Sortable Taxonomies” setting.
If you are using WooCommerce, you need to decide if you enable “Product categories” under Sortable Taxonomies, because WooCommerce already has its own drag-and-drop ordering interface for product categories. If you enable this setting, you’re using Intuitive CPO’s drag-and-drop ordering interface, and if not, you’re using the default WooCommerce one.

If you are not using WooCommerce, or if you are using WooCommerce and you **enabled “Product categories”** under “Sortable Taxonomies”, add the following code snippet to your (child) theme’s functions.php to fix facet ordering when using “Term order”:

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More info// Use if not using WooCommerce, 
// or if so, if "Product categories" is enabled under Sortable Taxonomies.
add_filter( 'get_terms_args', function( $args, $taxonomies ) {
  if ( isset( $args['term_order'] ) ) {
    $args['orderby'] = 'term_order';
  }
  return $args;
}, 10, 2 );

add_filter( 'get_terms_orderby', function( $orderby, $query_vars, $taxonomies ) {
  return 'term_order' === $query_vars['orderby'] ? 't.term_order' : $orderby;
}, 10, 3 );
```

If you are using WooCommerce, and you **disabled “Product categories”** under “Sortable Taxonomies”, add the following snippet instead. It fixes taxonomy ordering for all taxonomies like the above snippet, but preserves WooCommerce drag-and-drop ordering for product categories (which happens by a term meta field named “order”):

```
How to use custom PHP code?PHP code can be added to your (child) theme's functions.php file. Alternatively, you can use the Custom Hooks add-on, or a code snippets plugin. More info// Use if "Product categories" is disabled under Sortable Taxonomies.
add_filter( 'get_terms_args', function( $args, $taxonomies ) {
  if ( isset( $args['term_order'] ) && 'order' !== $args['meta_key'] ) {
    $args['orderby'] = 'term_order';
  }
  return $args;
}, 10, 2 );

add_filter( 'get_terms_orderby', function( $orderby, $query_vars, $taxonomies ) {
  return 'term_order' === $query_vars['orderby'] ? 't.term_order' : $orderby;
}, 10, 3 );
```
5. Next, order your taxonomy terms by dragging them into place in the term list screen(s).
6. Last but not least: [do a full re-index](/help-center/indexing/#how-to-run-the-indexer).
Important:Remember to **re-index when you change the hierarchy of terms** (e.g., changing to another parent). Re-indexing when changing terms for posts is not necessary; this [happens automatically](/help-center/indexing/#automatic-indexing). (See [this list for when to re-index](/help-center/indexing/#when-to-re-index)).
